  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserHalfWidth_double,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserHalfWidth_rate,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserPair_fst,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserPair_snd,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserPair_weighted}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-condenser-output-transport,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-programs}
  The actual output length is exactly $2H=cb$, so the paired map needs
  no padding. Its half-width satisfies
  $u(2H)\le(u+1)k+ub$. For positive rate parameter $u$, the paired map is
  a weighted strong condenser from threshold $2^k$ to threshold $2^k$
  with error at most $2^{-e}$, over two half-alphabets of size $2^H$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The even field width gives the length identity and transfers the
  existing output-rate bound. The fixed-length serialization followed
  by splitting is injective. Apply output transport to the proved
  weighted guarantee of the decoded scheduled condenser.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite splitting budgets for the actual condenser]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-split-budget}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserHalfWidth_capacity,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserPair_split_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserPair_split_budget_of_bound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitCondenserPair_next_entropy}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-condenser}
  Write $H$ for the actual output half-width and $b$ for the field bit
  width. For $u>0$, the coordinate capacity gives $k\le2H$. For $u>1$,
  the sufficient integer budget
  \[
    u\bigl(b+2(s+E)\bigr)\le(u-1)k
  \]
  implies $s\le H$ and $H+s+E\le k$. Here the splitting exponent $E$
  can differ from the condenser error exponent. One may replace $b$ in
  this sufficient condition by its proved upper bound. Taking the budget
  at $s=0$ justifies the maximal next entropy $k-H-E$, with exact equality
  $H+(k-H-E)+E=k$ and no truncated-subtraction loss.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The chosen coordinate count gives capacity, since its powering width is
  at most the field width. Add the budget to the output-rate inequality
  and cancel the positive factor $2u$ to obtain the entropy inequality.
  Capacity then gives $s\le H$. Prove $H+E\le k$ before using subtraction.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[A runtime program for one shared-seed block level]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitBlockCondenserBits}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-condenser-program}
  Given a word of concatenated $n$-bit blocks, a seed word, a unary block
  count, and the condenser parameters, apply the scheduled bit program
  to each fixed-width input slice using the same seed and concatenate
  the resulting output words in block order.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform computation and exact layout of one block level]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitBlockCondenserBits_length,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitBlockCondenserBits_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitBlockCondenserBits_ofFn}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-program}
  The block program is polynomial-time when its numerical parameters have
  uniform unary certificates and its words and unary block count are
  polynomial-time. Its exact output length is the block count times the
  component output length. On a concatenated fixed-width tuple, its output
  is exactly the concatenation of the component outputs. Zero blocks and
  zero block width are included.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Use polynomial-time fixed-width slicing and the actual condenser
  certificate inside finite-range concatenation. Each component has
  the same exact length. Slicing the concatenated fixed-width input
  recovers its corresponding tuple entry.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The runtime block program computes the statistical level]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-correctness}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.explicitBlockCondenserBits_eq_condenseSplitMap}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-program,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-condense-split-level}
  On a concatenated tuple of fixed-width Boolean blocks and the canonical
  encoding of a field seed, the block program returns exactly the
  concatenated output of the statistical condense-and-split map. Each
  pair's first half immediately precedes its second half. The equality
  includes zero blocks and zero input width, without entropy assumptions.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the componentwise program identity, recombine each actual paired
  output, and identify the flattening order of the split tuple.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Maximal coupling and correlated marginal replacement]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-coupling}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_maximal_coupling,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_marginal_replacement_kernel,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_marginal_replacement_coupling,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_joint_marginal_replacement}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weight-distance}
  Any two finite probability weights admit a coupling with disagreement
  probability equal to their statistical distance. For a joint law
  $p(a,b)$ and a proposed first marginal $q(a)$, there is a joint law $p'$
  with first marginal $q$, unchanged second marginal, and
  $d(p,p')=d(p_A,q)$. The lifted law $\rho((a,b),a')$ also satisfies
  \[
    p_A(a)\rho((a,b),a')=p(a,b)\sum_{b'}\rho((a,b'),a'),
  \]
  including rows with zero mass. This records the conditional independence
  used in the finite coupling ingredient of Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao,
  Lemma 4.19, which credits Li's 2015 Lemma 3.20. The full extension over
  an external conditioning variable is a further obligation.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Place common pointwise mass on the diagonal and couple the residual
  weights by their normalized product. Lift this coupling through the old
  conditional row weights. Projection bounds the joint distance from
  above by disagreement, and marginalization gives the reverse bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Finite block sources]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.blockPrefix,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.blockPrefixWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-probability-weights}
  A threshold-$K$ source on $t$ blocks is a normalized probability weight
  on $\alpha^t$ for which every one-block prefix extension satisfies
  $Kp(u,a)\le p(u)$. This multiplicative definition includes zero-mass
  prefixes without conditional division. For $K>0$ it expresses conditional
  min-entropy at least $\log_2K$ in every block given its predecessors.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Block-source structure]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source-structure}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.mono,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.prefix,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.mixture,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.capped,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.eq_uniform,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.isBlockSource_zero_iff,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.isBlockSource_one_iff,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.isBlockSource_two_iff}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source}
  Block-source caps survive taking prefixes, decreasing the threshold,
  and taking normalized nonnegative mixtures. Zero blocks impose only
  normalization; one block gives the ordinary point-mass cap. Two blocks
  are equivalent to a capped first marginal and the inequalities
  $Kp(a,b)\le p_A(a)$ for the second block. A source on $t$ blocks has
  joint threshold $K^t$. At threshold $|\alpha|$, the joint source is
  exactly uniform on all block tuples, including the zero-block case.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Prefix pushforwards compose and commute with finite linear combinations.
  Apply these identities to the defining nonnegative linear inequalities.
  The zero-, one-, and two-block cases use the corresponding tuple
  equivalences and explicit marginal sums. Induction on prefix length
  gives the joint cap; the full-cardinality cap forces uniformity.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Two-block repair]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-block-repair}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoBlockRepair}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-probability-weights}
  For a joint law $p(a,b)$ with point weights at most $\mu$, replace each
  row of mass less than $K\mu$ by a uniform row of the same total mass.
  Keep every other row unchanged.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Marginal-preserving two-block splitting]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-block-repair}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoBlockRepair_dist_le_low_mass,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoBlockRepair_capped,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_twoBlock_repair,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_twoBlock_repair_pow_two,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_twoBlock_repair_pow_two_capped}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-block-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source-structure}
  Let each of two blocks have $2^m$ possible values, and let $p$ have
  point-mass cap $2^{-k}$. If $t\le m$ and $m+t+e\le k$, there is a
  two-block source with threshold $2^t$, the same first marginal, and
  distance at most $2^{-e}$ from $p$. More generally, if $0<K\le|\beta|$
  and $K|\beta|\mu\le1$, the repair has first-marginal and conditional
  threshold $K$, and distance at most $|\alpha|K\mu$. A sharper bound is
  the total mass of the repaired rows. The explicit repair also preserves
  every original joint point-mass cap. This is the finite two-block step
  in Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao, Lemma 5.3, attributed there to
  Goldreich--Wigderson (1997).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Uniformizing a row preserves its mass and enforces its conditional cap;
  an unrepaired row already has mass at least $K\mu$. The marginal cap
  follows by summing the original point bound. The absolute discrepancy
  in a repaired row is at most twice its mass. For the power-of-two
  specialization, $2^m2^t/2^k\le2^{-e}$ supplies both budgets. Averaging a
  row cannot increase its largest point weight, preserving the old joint cap.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact distance averaging with tags and seeds]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_tagged_mixture,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_mixture_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_seedFamilyWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_weightedSeededOutput_seedFamilyWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.mapWeight_tagged}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weight-distance,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-strong}
  For nonnegative coefficients $w_i$, retaining a component tag gives
  \[
    d\bigl((i,x)\mapsto w_ip_i(x),(i,x)\mapsto w_iq_i(x)\bigr)
      =\sum_iw_i d(p_i,q_i).
  \]
  Forgetting the tag gives the corresponding upper bound. Thus the
  distance between two uniform-seed families equals the average of their
  conditional distances. The component weights need not be normalized.
  A deterministic map that preserves a tag acts separately on its components.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Expand the finite sum defining distance and factor the nonnegative
  coefficient out of each absolute value. Contraction gives the untagged
  inequality. Uniform-seed averaging uses coefficient $1/|\mathrm{Seed}|$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Conditional weights and independent uniform extension]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-weights}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.conditionalWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.uniformExtensionWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.uniformSecondWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-probability-weights}
  For a joint law $p(a,b)$ with first marginal $p_A(a)$, its conditional
  row is $p(a,b)/p_A(a)$ when $p_A(a)\ne0$ and uniform otherwise.
  Uniform extension appends independent uniform randomness to a supplied
  first-coordinate law. Write $U_2p(a,b)=p_A(a)/|B|$ for the law retaining
  the actual first marginal and uniformizing its second coordinate.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact conditional distance and processing side information]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.conditionalWeight_factor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsProbabilityWeight.conditionalWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsProbabilityWeight.uniformSecond,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_uniformSecond_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_uniformSecond_tagged,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.firstWeight_map_fiber,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_uniformSecond_map_first_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-weights,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  On a nonempty second alphabet, conditional rows of a probability law
  are normalized, including null rows. Multiplying each row by its
  marginal recovers the original law, and
  \[
    d(p,U_2p)=\sum_a p_A(a)d(p(\cdot\mid a),U_B).
  \]
  Processing the second coordinate within each row leaves the first
  marginal unchanged. Deterministically processing the retained first
  coordinate cannot increase distance from an independent uniform output.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A nonnegative zero-mass row vanishes pointwise. On positive rows cancel
  the marginal, then use exact tagged-distance averaging. Uniformization
  commutes with deterministic processing of the first coordinate, so
  contraction proves the last claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Sharp bounded expectations and strong extraction in distance]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bounded-expectations}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightExpectation_sub_le_dist,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightedStrongSeededExtractor_iff_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weight-distance,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-strong}
  For equal-mass real weights $p,q$ and $0\le f\le1$,
  $|\sum_xp(x)f(x)-\sum_xq(x)f(x)|\le d(p,q)$.
  On nonempty seed and output alphabets, the weighted strong-extractor
  test contract is equivalent to distance at most $\varepsilon$ from the
  independent uniform seed-output law for every normalized capped source.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The positive and negative parts of $p-q$ have equal total mass, each
  equal to its total variation. A statistic in $[0,1]$ is bounded by
  these parts. The extractor equivalence uses the equal-mass finite-test
  characterization of total variation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Strong extraction with an average leakage budget]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-average-leakage}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.leakage_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.leakage_average_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bounded-expectations}
  Let $E$ be a weighted strong extractor with threshold $K$ and
  nonnegative error $\varepsilon$, on nonempty seed and output alphabets.
  If $p(t,x)$ is a probability law and $0\le p(t,x)\le\mu(t)$, extraction
  from $x$ with an independent uniform seed has distance at most
  $\varepsilon+K\sum_t\mu(t)$ from uniform, retaining both the entire
  tag $t$ and the seed. No cap on every conditional row is assumed.
  This is the finite average-entropy step in Chattopadhyay--Liao,
  Lemma~3.26, which credits Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao and ideas from
  Chattopadhyay--Li (2016).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A positive row with $K\mu(t)\le p_T(t)$ satisfies the extractor's
  conditional cap. Other rows have total mass at most
  $K\sum_t\mu(t)$ and contribute distance at most their mass. Null
  rows contribute zero. Sum the row distances and average over the seed.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Factored sources and two-sided extraction laws]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.leakageSourceWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoSidedExtractionWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-weights}
  A shared transcript has law $w(z)$, and the left and right variables
  have conditionally independent laws $l_z(a),r_z(b)$. The source
  $x(z,a)$ and observation $u(z,a)$ depend on the left side; the seed
  $y(z,b)$ and observation $v(z,b)$ depend on the right. A further
  finite leak $L(z,v,a)$ may depend on the left variable and the right
  observation together. The actual output law retains
  $(z,b,u(z,a),L(z,v(z,b),a))$ alongside $E(x(z,a),y(z,b))$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Extraction with two-sided finite leakage]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoSidedExtractionWeight_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.twoSidedExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.two_sided_leakage_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.two_sided_leakage_dist_le_of_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-average-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bounded-expectations}
  Let $w,l_z,r_z$ be probability laws and $E$ a threshold-$K$ strong
  extractor of nonnegative error $\varepsilon$, with nonempty seed and
  output alphabets. Suppose a nonnegative envelope $\mu(z,u)$ bounds
  $\Pr[Z=z,U=u,X=x]$ for every $x$, and the law of $(Z,V,Y)$ is within
  $\delta$ of its actual $(Z,V)$ marginal with an independent uniform
  seed. The actual retained extraction law has distance at most
  \[
    \varepsilon+\delta+K|\mathcal L|\sum_{z,u}\mu(z,u)
  \]
  from its actual retained marginal with an independent uniform output,
  where $\mathcal L$ is the extra leak's alphabet. A bound
  $K|\mathcal L|\sum\mu\le\rho$ gives error $\varepsilon+\delta+\rho$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  For the independent uniform seed, apply the average leakage theorem
  with tag $(z,v,u,L)$. Refining an event reduces its point mass;
  averaging over $v$ leaves envelope total $|\mathcal L|\sum\mu$.
  Conditional independence expresses both actual and uniform-seed
  distances as expectations of the same $[0,1]$-valued row statistic.
  The bounded-expectation inequality therefore charges the seed
  discrepancy once, while retaining all of the right variable.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Actual merging after selected observations and a prefix]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-merging}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.SmoothMergingTranscript,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothMergingTranscript,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothMergingSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothTwoSidedExtractionWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  From the original factored law, retain
  $H=((Z,U(Z,A)),Q(Z,A))$. The right seed $Y(H,B)$ and right
  observation $V(H,B)$ may depend on this entire transcript.
  The extraction law keeps $H$, the complete original right variable
  $B$, and a leak $W(H,V(H,B),A)$ alongside $E(X(Z,A),Y(H,B))$.
  Both $Q$ and $W$ are evaluated on the original left variable.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Smooth-source two-sided merging]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-merging}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothMergingSeedWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothTwoSidedExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.smooth_two_sided_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothMergingSeedWeight_prefix_dist,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.smooth_two_sided_dist_le_of_prefix_seed}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-merging,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-factored-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  Let the original factors be probability laws and let $E$ be a
  threshold-$K$ strong extractor of nonnegative error $\varepsilon$, with
  nonempty seed and output alphabets. Suppose $(Z,U,X)$ is within
  $\rho$ of its actual $(Z,U)$ marginal times an independent uniform $X$,
  and $(H,V,Y)$ has analogous seed discrepancy at most $\delta$.
  The actual retained extraction law has error at most
  \[
    \varepsilon+\delta+\rho+K\,|W|\,|Q|/|X|.
  \]
  No pointwise conditional source cap or repaired witness is assumed.
  When $V,Y$ depend only on $(Z,Q)$, their joint seed discrepancy is
  exactly unchanged by retaining $U$, so the seed premise needs only
  $(Z,Q,V)$. This is the smooth-source consumer for
  Chattopadhyay--Liao Theorem~6.1; the actual one-round specialization
  is proved below.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Observe $U$, then repair the honest source coordinate to uniform
  while preserving the entire original conditional left variable.
  Observing the actual $Q$ gives joint envelope total $|Q|/|X|$.
  Apply two-sided merging. The original $U,Q,B,W$ marginal is exact,
  so transfer costs the old distance once. If the seed uses only
  $(Z,Q)$, finite weighted averaging sums out the independent extra
  left observation, including null rows.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual independence-merging output law]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-independence-merging}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.independenceMergingWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  Given transcript $Z$, let the entire left tuple
  $(X,X_1,\ldots,X_t)$ be independent of the entire right tuple
  $(Y,Y_1,\ldots,Y_t)$. For index sets $S,T\subseteq[t]$, the actual
  law applies $E$ to each source-seed pair and retains
  $(Z,Y,Y_1,\ldots,Y_t,(E(X_i,Y_i))_{i\in S\cup T})$ alongside
  $E(X,Y)$. It is the deterministic image of the factored source law,
  and $S,T$ may overlap.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite independence merging with the average entropy budget]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-independence-merging}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.independence_merging_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.independence_merging_dist_le_of_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-independence-merging,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  Let the transcript and both conditional tuple laws be normalized, and
  let $E$ be a threshold-$K$ strong extractor of nonnegative error
  $\varepsilon$, with nonempty seed and output alphabets. Suppose
  $\mu(z,u)\ge0$ bounds every joint mass
  $\Pr[Z=z,X_T=u,X=x]$, and $(Z,Y_S,Y)$ is within $\delta$ of its
  actual $(Z,Y_S)$ marginal with an independent uniform seed.
  The independence-merging law is within
  \[
    \varepsilon+\delta+K|\mathrm{Out}|^{|S|}\sum_{z,u}\mu(z,u)
  \]
  of its actual retained marginal with a fresh independent uniform
  output. If the last term is at most $\varepsilon$, the error is
  $2\varepsilon+\delta$. This is the finite envelope form of
  Chattopadhyay--Liao, Lemma~3.26; the envelope total expresses the
  average conditional guessing budget without logarithms.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply two-sided leakage with $U=X_T$, $V=Y_S$, and the extra leak
  $(E(X_i,Y_i))_{i\in S}$, whose alphabet has size
  $|\mathrm{Out}|^{|S|}$. The retained source copies $X_T$ and all
  right-side seeds recover every remaining output indexed by $T$.
  On overlaps use the already retained $S$ output. Processing side
  information preserves the actual uniform comparison and contracts
  distance. The numerical budget gives the stated final error.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Transcript updates after deterministic messages]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptKernel}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-weights,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  Given transcript law $w(z)$, source kernel $p_z(a)$, and deterministic
  message $f(z,a)$, the new transcript law is
  $w'(z,u)=w(z)\Pr[f(z,A)=u\mid z]$. Its updated source kernel is the
  conditional law of $A$ on that message, completed uniformly on null
  rows. Both original source coordinates remain available after updating.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact factorization after adaptive observations]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptKernel_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptWeight_mul_kernel,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_observe_left,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_observe_right,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_observe_left_right,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_left_right_probability}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  A left-side observation updates its conditional kernel while preserving
  the right kernel; a right-side observation has the symmetric property.
  The resulting factored law is exactly the pushforward of the original
  law retaining the message and both original side variables. Every
  updated kernel and transcript is normalized. A left message followed
  by a right message depending on that first message has the same exact
  factorization. These rules include null observation rows and formalize
  the deterministic-conditioning step of Chattopadhyay--Liao, Lemma~3.25.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Multiplying the updated kernel by its transcript weight recovers the
  original joint mass on the observed event and is zero elsewhere.
  Finite pushforward sums then identify the complete factored law.
  Compose the two exact pushforwards for the adaptive observation.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Marginal replacement preserving an external transcript]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-coupling}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_conditional_marginal_replacement_kernel,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_conditional_joint_marginal_replacement}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-coupling,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  Let $p$ be a probability law of $(Z,A,B)$ and let $q$ be a probability
  law of $(Z,A')$ with the same $Z$ marginal. There is a coupling
  preserving the complete original $(Z,A,B)$ law, giving $(Z,A')$ law
  $q$, and satisfying
  $\Pr[A\ne A']=d(p_{ZA},q)$. Its kernel makes $A'$ independent of
  $B$ given $(Z,A)$, including null rows. The projected law $p'$ of
  $(Z,A',B)$ preserves $(Z,B)$ jointly and satisfies
  $d(p,p')=d(p_{ZA},q)$. No per-transcript error bound is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Normalize each transcript row, lift a maximal marginal coupling
  through its original conditional kernel, and multiply by the common
  transcript mass. Summing exact row disagreements gives the global
  joint marginal distance. Projection gives the upper bound on joint
  distance; marginal contraction gives the matching lower bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Tag-dependent output bijections preserve uniformity distance]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-output-bijections}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.mapWeight_uniformSecond_fiberEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_uniformSecond_fiberEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.mapWeight_uniformSecond_add_right,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_uniformSecond_add_right}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  For arbitrary finite real weights $p(a,b)$ and output equivalences
  $e_a:B\simeq C$, the map $(a,b)\mapsto(a,e_a(b))$ commutes with
  uniformizing the output and preserves the exact distance $d(p,U_2p)$.
  Adding a tag-dependent mask in a finite additive group is a special case.
  The statements require neither normalization nor nonnegative weights.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Each fiber bijection preserves its cardinality and uniform law, while
  the first marginal is unchanged. Apply deterministic contraction in
  both directions of the complete tagged equivalence.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Retained affine extraction laws]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedExtractionWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineExtractionWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  Sample $(Z,A,B)$ from a factored law. A left source $x(Z,A)$ and
  right seed $y(Z,B)$ give the retained law of
  $(Z,B,E(x(Z,A),y(Z,B)))$. The affine version first combines the
  left source with a right mask $m(Z,B)$ using a specified operation,
  and applies the same extractor and actual seed. Both are deterministic
  images of the original factored law and retain the whole right variable.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Affine initial extraction with complete right information]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineExtractionWeight_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.retained_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.affine_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-output-bijections}
  Let the factor laws be normalized and $E$ be a threshold-$K$ strong
  extractor of nonnegative error $\varepsilon$, with nonempty seed and
  output alphabets. Suppose $\mu(z)\ge0$ bounds every joint source mass
  $\Pr[Z=z,x(Z,A)=x]$, and $(Z,y(Z,B))$ is within $\delta$ of its
  actual transcript law with a fresh uniform seed. Retained extraction
  has distance at most $\varepsilon+\delta+K\sum_z\mu(z)$ from its
  actual $(Z,B)$ marginal with independent uniform output. The same bound
  holds on the combined input whenever each fixed mask and seed acts
  bijectively on the extractor output. The mask may be correlated
  arbitrarily with the seed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Specialize two-sided leakage to singleton observation and leakage
  alphabets, retaining all of $B$. For the masked input, the extractor's
  equivariance identifies its actual law with a tagged output bijection
  of the unmasked law. Exact distance preservation adds no error.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The actual scheduled program on a Boolean affine source]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-affine-initialization}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockBooleanExtractor_affine_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockBooleanExtractor_dyadic_affine_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-boolean}
  The actual scheduled Boolean extractor, under its proved finite
  budget and reserve conditions, has the affine retained-law guarantee
  with error $\varepsilon_{\rm schedule}+\delta+K\sum\mu$ on a left
  source XOR a right mask. The explicit dyadic schedule gives
  $\varepsilon_{\rm schedule}=2^{-e}$. These are the same bit programs
  and exact Boolean seed widths already supplied by the uniform runtime
  construction. No extractor or finite-field instance is a caller premise.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Use the proved weighted extraction guarantee and fixed-seed XOR law
  of the actual program. Coordinatewise XOR with the mask output is
  an involution on the complete block-output tuple. Apply affine initial
  extraction, preserving the actual full right-side marginal.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite parameters at bounded extraction depth]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bounded-depth}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedBits_boundedDepth_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_depth24_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_depth64_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-boolean}
  If $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L$, $64\le L$, $h\le64$, and $E\le L$,
  the actual scheduled extractor with reserve $4096(L+E+1)$ and leaf
  output width $L$ has seed length at most $2^{24}L$. For $1\le L$ and
  $E\le L$, its source entropy in bits is at most $2^{62}L$ at depth 24,
  and at most $2^{142}L$ at depth 64. These are conservative finite bounds
  on the existing construction, supporting successive calls with matched
  seed and output widths; they assert no correlation-breaker guarantee.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound the existing seed formula using $h+1\le65$ and
  $\lceil\log_2(L+E+1)\rceil\le L+E$.
  Substitute the reserve in the exact initial entropy $4^hQ$ for each
  of the two fixed depths and use $L+E+1\le3L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[An actual extractor with matched seed and output widths]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockSeedBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockOutputBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-program}
  Set $d=2^{24}L$ and output width $2^hL$.
  On Boolean vectors of source width $n$ and seed width $d$, run the
  scheduled bit program with $E=e+h+2$ and reserve $4096(L+E+1)$.
  Feed exactly its prescribed seed prefix, completed by zero bits when
  necessary, and read the actual output in flat block order.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Strong extraction with the entire padded seed retained]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_ofFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_ofFn_take,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_xor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_seedBits_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_depth24,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_depth64}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bounded-depth,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-boolean}
  Suppose $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L$, $64\le L$, $h\le64$, and
  $e+h+2\le L$. The prescribed seed fits within $d$ bits, and the actual
  extractor has strong error $2^{-e}$ at its exact scheduled entropy
  threshold, retaining all $d$ seed bits. At depths 24 and 64 the source
  entropy may be weakened to $2^{62}L$ and $2^{142}L$ bits respectively.
  Fixed-seed source XOR becomes coordinatewise output XOR for all parameters.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Identify the raw bit output with the scheduled Boolean tuple, flatten
  by a coordinate equivalence, and pad the seed while retaining its
  unused suffix. Apply the finite depth bounds to the exact entropy formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Matched extraction at growing depth]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-growing}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_growing_seedBits_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_growing_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_growing_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.matchedBlockExtractor_growing,

  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-boolean}
  Suppose
  \[
    \lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L,\qquad e+h+2\le L,\qquad
    (h+1)(e+2h+\lceil\log_2(L+1)\rceil+4)\le L.
  \]
  The actual matched extractor at depth $h$ has seed width $2^{24}L$,
  output width $2^hL$, and strong error $2^{-e}$ at its exact scheduled
  entropy threshold, retaining the entire padded seed. Its entropy
  requirement may be weakened to $2^{2h+14}L$ bits. Nonvacuous use still
  requires a source meeting this mass bound. For $e\le L$, depth
  $h=\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil+64$ also gives output width at least
  $2^{62}L+(2t+2)2^{24}L+e$.
  The logarithmic-depth runtime below implements the depth choice
  $h=\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil+64$ with polynomially many blocks in $t$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound the exact scheduled seed formula by the displayed depth budget,
  then use retained-seed padding. Bound the reserve in the initial
  entropy $4^hQ$. The ceiling-logarithm inequality gives the final
  output-width reserve.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Matched extraction with a correlated right mask]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-affine}

  given $Z$, with a nonnegative honest-left joint envelope $\mu$ and
  right-derived seed discrepancy at most $\delta$. XOR the source with
  an arbitrary mask from the complete right state. Under the preceding
  growing-depth budget, the actual matched output retains that state
  and has distance from independent uniform output at most
  \[
    2^{-e}+\delta+2^{\,2^{2h+14}L}\sum_z\mu(z).
  \]
  At depth 64, the bounded-depth conditions give the same estimate
  with threshold $2^{\,2^{142}L}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The actual fixed-seed XOR identity makes the mask an output translation
  depending only on the retained right state. Apply the finite affine
  extraction theorem to the corresponding checked matched extractor.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The concrete look-ahead and advice-bit step]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-flipflop-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.flipFlopSeedPrefix,

  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-runtime}
  Use the actual depth-24 extractor for $W$ and $Q$, and the actual
  depth-64 extractor $R$ for refreshing from the original right source $y$.
  A look-ahead returns $(r_1,r_2)$ from
  $s_1=\operatorname{prefix}_d(q)$, $r_1=W(x,s_1)$,
  $s_2=Q(q,r_1)$, $r_2=W(x,s_2)$.
  For advice zero, refresh with $R(y,r_1)$, perform another look-ahead,
  and return $R(y,\bar r_2)$; for advice one, use $r_2$ and $\bar r_1$.
  This is Chattopadhyay--Goyal--Li Algorithm 1, with all eight calls explicit.
\end{definition}

  canonical input computes exactly the vector step for either advice bit.
  Its output always has at most $2^{64}L$ bits, including invalid inputs.
  The complete step estimates and full advice runtime are formalized below.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok

\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Source envelopes after transcript messages]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-transcript-envelopes}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscriptWeight_nonnegative,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_left_source_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_left_envelope,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_left_envelope_sum,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_right_envelope,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_right_envelope_sum,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_left_right_envelope,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedTranscript_left_right_envelope_sum}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  Suppose the original law factors into nonnegative left and right kernels
  given $Z$, and $\mu(z)$ bounds each original joint mass of the left source
  with transcript $z$. A left message in $U$ admits the envelope
  $\mu'(z,u)=\mu(z)$, of total $|U|\sum_z\mu(z)$. A right message
  admits $\mu'(z,v)=\mu(z)\Pr[V=v\mid z]$, whose total is unchanged
  when the right kernels have mass one. A left message followed by an
  adaptive right message pays only the factor $|U|$. The updated source
  masses are exactly the original joint message-and-source masses;
  no positive-probability or per-row entropy premise is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Sum the exact transcript-weight times conditional-kernel identity over
  each source fiber. Refining a nonnegative event can only reduce mass.
  The opposite side's conditional message probabilities sum to one.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Actual alternating extraction laws]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-laws}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.alternatingSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.alternatingExtractionWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineAlternatingWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  From the original factored law of $(Z,A,B)$, reveal $v(Z,B)$ and
  compute a seed $s(Z,v(Z,B),A)$. Alternating extraction applies $E$
  to the right source and this seed, retaining $(Z,v(Z,B),A)$.
  The affine two-call law instead obtains that seed by applying a first
  extractor to the combined left source and right mask. It retains the
  whole left state and the first call's seed and extracted mask.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Alternating extraction retaining the opposite source]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.alternatingExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.alternating_joint_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.alternating_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-laws,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-transcript-envelopes,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization}
  Let the original factors be probability laws and let $E$ have threshold
  $K$ and nonnegative error $\varepsilon$, with nonempty seed and output
  alphabets. Suppose the actual seed is $\delta$-close to uniform jointly
  with $(Z,V)$, and $\mu(z,v)\ge0$ bounds the joint right-source masses
  with that transcript. Then the actual alternating output, with all of
  $(Z,V,A)$ retained, has distance at most
  $\varepsilon+\delta+K\sum_{z,v}\mu(z,v)$ from its actual retained
  marginal with uniform output. An original source envelope $\mu(z)$
  instead gives $\varepsilon+\delta+K|V|\sum_z\mu(z)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Update the transcript by the right message and swap the two sides of
  its exact factored law. The envelope identity gives the new source cap.
  Apply retained extraction; both the next seed and output laws are
  deterministic images of the original distribution. Only one copy of
  the incoming seed discrepancy is charged.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[An affine extraction seeds the next alternating call]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-alternation}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineAlternatingWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.affine_alternating_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-extraction,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-initialization}
  Let the first extractor $E:X\times S\to M$ have threshold $K$ and
  error $\varepsilon$, and the second $F:Y\times M\to O$ threshold
  $L$ and error $\eta$, both nonnegative. Suppose fixed-mask output
  transport is an equivalence determined by the extracted mask, the
  original factors are normalized, the left and right joint source
  envelopes are nonnegative $\mu,\nu$, and the first seed is
  $\delta$-close to uniform jointly with $Z$. For nonempty seed and output
  alphabets, the actual two-call law has distance at most
  \[
    \eta+\varepsilon+\delta+K\sum_z\mu(z)
      +L|S\times M|\sum_z\nu(z)
  \]
  from its actual retained marginal with uniform final output. It retains
  the complete left state, first seed, and extracted right mask.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Reveal the first seed and its extracted mask. The first actual output
  is now a function of the left state and this transcript. Project its
  retained affine guarantee to obtain the next seed bound and apply the
  alternating transition. The right-message alphabet is exactly $S\times M$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Actual tampered two-round look-ahead laws]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-laws}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadExtractionWeight}

  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-laws}
  From the original law $w(z)l_z(a)r_z(b)$ compute
  $s_1=p(q)$, $r_1=W(x,s_1)$, $s_2=Q(q,r_1)$, and $r_2=W(x,s_2)$,
  and the same calls on arbitrarily correlated tampered values $x',q'$.
  The seed law retains $z,s_1,s_1',a$ alongside $s_2$.
  The output law retains $z,b,r_1,r_1'$ alongside $r_2$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Two-round extraction with the tampered transcript retained]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadSeedWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadExtractionWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.lookAhead_seed_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.lookAhead_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.lookAhead_dist_le_of_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-laws,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-extraction,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-transcript-envelopes}
  Assume normalized factored sides, nonnegative original joint source
  envelopes $\mu(z),\nu(z)$, and initial honest-seed discrepancy $\delta$
  jointly with $Z$. Let $W:X\times S\to M$ and $Q:Q_0\times M\to S$
  have thresholds $K,J$ and nonnegative errors $\varepsilon,\eta$.
  The actual final law differs from its retained marginal with an
  independent uniform final output by at most
  \[
    2\varepsilon+\eta+\delta+
    K(1+|M\times M|)\sum_z\mu(z)+J|S\times S|\sum_z\nu(z).
  \]
  The retained variables determine both initial and both second seeds.
  No tampered seed is assumed uniform, and no per-transcript entropy
  bound is required. This formalizes the two-round case of
  Chattopadhyay--Goyal--Li Lemma 6.5 and Claim 6.6 with explicit losses.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Extract $r_1$ retaining the full right state. Observe $(s_1,s_1')$ on
  the right, switch sides, and derive the second honest seed estimate.
  Observe $(r_1,r_1')$ on the left, then extract the final left output.
  Exact transcript factorizations and average envelope sums charge the
  two message alphabets and include null rows without case assumptions.
\end{proof}

\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The actual matched program satisfies the two-round bound]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-concrete-lookahead}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.flipFlopLookAheadWeight_eq,

  the actual matched program has distance at most
  \[

    \qquad K=2^{2^{62}L},\quad M=2^{2\cdot2^{24}L}.

\begin{theorem}[Uniform coordinate repair preserving the original sources]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-factored-repair}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.rightCoordinateLift_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.factoredWeight_rightCoordinateLift,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_factored_uniform_right_repair,

  For a normalized factored law $w(z)l_z(a)r_z(b)$ and right coordinate
  $q(z,b)\in Q$, there is a normalized replacement kernel on $(B,Q)$.
  Its $B$ marginal is unchanged and its $Q$ marginal is uniform given $Z$.
  The full factored replacement has distance exactly
  $d((Z,q),(Z,U_Q))$ from the original deterministic lift.
  The entire original $(Z,A,B)$ law and all source envelopes are preserved.
  The new $Q$ coordinate may remain correlated with $B$.
  Transferring conditional uniformity through a later deterministic map
  costs at most twice this repair distance, or once if its retained
  marginal is unchanged.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply finite marginal replacement to each normalized right row, keeping
  its original right coordinate. Average the exact row distances against
  $w$. Deterministic contraction and the triangle inequality give the
  continuation bounds, including null rows and empty alphabets.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Look-ahead from a nearly uniform right state]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-uniform-state}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedWeight_image_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedWeight_uniform_image,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedWeight_uniform_image_dist,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.lookAhead_uniformState_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.flipFlopSeedPrefix_uniform,

  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-factored-repair,

    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-concrete-lookahead}
  Suppose the whole right state is $\rho$-close to uniform jointly with
  $Z$, and its initial-seed map sends uniform states to uniform seeds.
  Under the original left-source envelope $\mu$, the actual two-round law
  retaining the original right state and both first outputs has error
  \[
    2\varepsilon+\eta+2\rho+
    K(1+|M|^2)\sum_z\mu(z)+J|S|^2/|Q|.
  \]
  No pointwise entropy bound for the approximately uniform state is needed.
  For the actual matched program, the prefix map is balanced and the bound
  is $3\cdot2^{-e}+2\rho+K(1+D^2)\sum_z\mu(z)+KD^2/C$, where

  same finite scale conditions as the concrete two-round theorem.

\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Repair the distinguished state, use envelope $w(z)/|Q|$ and an exactly
  uniform initial seed, then apply two-round extraction. Project away the
  auxiliary repaired coordinate and transfer back at cost $2\rho$.
  A Boolean coordinate-splitting equivalence proves prefix uniformity.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The common look-ahead transcript and actual refresh laws]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-refresh}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.LookAheadBaseTranscript,

  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-laws}
  The common transcript records $Z$, both initial right states, and all
  four honest and tampered look-ahead outputs. The first refresh applies
  $R(y,r_1)$, retaining this transcript and the full left state.
  The second-output refresh applies $R(y,r_2)$ and additionally retains
  $R(y',r_1')$. Both laws are direct images of the original factored law.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Refresh estimates and exact source envelopes]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-refresh}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadBase_factored,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadBase_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadBase_left_envelope,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadBase_right_envelope,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadBaseLeftEnvelope_sum,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadBaseRightEnvelope_sum,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadFirstRefreshWeight_retainedSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadTamperedRefresh_factored,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.lookAhead_first_refresh_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.lookAhead_tampered_refresh_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-refresh,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-extraction,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-transcript-envelopes}
  Let $C,D,O$ be the cardinalities of the right state, look-ahead output,
  and refresh output. Write $K_W,K_R$ for the first and refresh thresholds,
  $\varepsilon_W,\varepsilon_R$ for their errors, and $\mu_X,\mu_Y$
  for original source envelopes. From the original seed error $\delta$,
  the first refresh has error at most
  \[
    \varepsilon_R+\varepsilon_W+\delta+
      K_W\sum\mu_X+K_RC^2\sum\mu_Y.
  \]
  If $E_{\mathrm{LA}}$ is the original two-round bound, the second-output
  refresh retaining the tampered refresh has error at most
  $\varepsilon_R+E_{\mathrm{LA}}+K_RC^2O\sum\mu_Y$.
  The common transcript admits exact normalized left and right kernels;
  their original-source envelope totals are $D^4\sum\mu_X$ and
  $C^2\sum\mu_Y$ respectively.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Observe the right state pair, derive the appropriate seed estimate from
  the actual first call or look-ahead, and alternate back to the right
  source. Append the remaining left messages using the retained full left
  state. Exact observation identities give the factorization and envelopes.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Refresh with a fixed-side tampered seed]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-fixed-tampering-refresh}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.fixedTampering_factored_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.fixedTampering_factors_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.fixedTamperingRefreshWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.fixedTamperingRefreshWeight_eq_factored,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.fixedTampering_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.fixedTampering_dist_le_of_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-extraction,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-transcript-envelopes}
  Let $v(Z,A)\in M$ be an arbitrary left-only tampered seed.
  The actual output $R(y,W(x,p(q)))$, retaining the full left state,
  $Z,v,q,R(y',v)$, and $W(x,p(q))$, has error at most
  \[
    \varepsilon_R+\varepsilon_W+\delta+
      K_W|M|\sum\mu_X+K_R|Q||O|\sum\mu_Y.
  \]
  Its only seed hypothesis is the original joint error $\delta$.
  The retained law has an exact normalized transcript factorization.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Reveal the left leak, extract retaining the whole right state, and
  reveal the right state and tampered refresh before the final alternating
  call. Charge each message to its source's original envelope.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Second-output refresh with a fixed-side tampered seed]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-fixed-tampering-second}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedWeight_observe_left_dist,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.fixedTamperingSecondRefreshWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.fixedTampering_second_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-fixed-tampering-refresh,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-extraction}
  A normalized left-only observation preserves the average original
  right-seed discrepancy exactly, including zero-mass observations.
  Let $v(Z,A)\in M$ be a left-only tampered seed. Refreshing from the
  actual second look-ahead output, while retaining $Z,v,q$, both honest
  look-ahead outputs, $R(y',v)$, and the full left state, has error at most
  \[
    \varepsilon_R+2\varepsilon_W+\varepsilon_Q+\delta+
      K_W|M|(1+|M|^2)\sum\mu_X+
      K_Q|S|^2\sum\mu_q+K_R|Q||O|\sum\mu_Y.
  \]
  All three envelopes and the prefix error $\delta$ concern the original
  factored law. This is a finite component of Chattopadhyay--Goyal--Li's
  already-distinct-advice analysis.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Reveal $v$ without changing the average seed error, apply two-round
  extraction, and observe the right state and tampered refresh before
  alternating to the original right source. The full left state and
  original right state reconstruct the retained honest history.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Either selected look-ahead refresh]

  common history and original left state. The tampered final refresh is
  unobserved; the two current right states may coincide.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Either refresh from a nearly uniform honest state]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-uniform-refresh}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.lookAheadSelectedRefreshWeight_probability,

  Suppose the honest right state is $\rho$-close to uniform given $Z$,
  and the initial-seed map sends uniform states to uniform seeds. For
  either refresh selection, the actual retained law has error at most
  \[
    \varepsilon_R+2\varepsilon_W+\varepsilon_Q+2\rho+
      K_W(1+|M|^2)\sum\mu_X+
      K_Q|S|^2/|Q|+K_R|Q|^2|O|\sum\mu_Y.

  Only the original left and right source envelopes are assumed. The

  This supplies the single-refresh step before advice first differs in

\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok

  marginal. Apply the selected-refresh estimate with exact uniform cap
  $w(z)/|Q|$ and transfer the complete retained law back at cost $2\rho$.
\end{proof}

  Apply the concrete eight-call step to both original source pairs with

  refreshed states, all eight look-ahead outputs, the tampered final output,
  and the full original left state. The honest final output and this
  transcript are a direct image of the original factored source law.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Complete honest-zero and tampered-one extraction]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-opposite-flipflop-false}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.flipFlopOpposite_false_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-opposite-flipflop,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-refresh,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-factored-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor}
  Under the common flip-flop size guard, suppose the original law factors
  into normalized sides given $Z$, with original left and right source
  envelopes $\mu,\nu$, and initial prefix error $\delta$. Let
  \[
    K=2^{2^{62}L},\quad J=2^{2^{142}L},\quad
    D=2^{2^{24}L},\quad C=2^{2^{64}L},\quad \varepsilon=2^{-e},
  \]
  and set $\rho=2\varepsilon+\delta+K\sum\mu+JC^2\sum\nu$.
  For honest advice zero and tampered advice one, the full actual law has
  distance from its retained marginal with uniform honest output at most
  \[
    2\rho+4\varepsilon+K(1+D^2)D^4\sum\mu+KD^2/C+JC^5\sum\nu.
  \]
  The tampered inputs may be arbitrarily correlated within each side.
  There is no entropy premise on the initial right state and no supplied
  intermediate-seed or extractor guarantee. The invariant across advice
  positions is constructed below.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Derive the first-refresh bound and observe the exact common transcript.
  Repair the refreshed coordinate while preserving both original sources.
  Apply the tampered second-output refresh theorem with a uniform repaired
  state and its balanced prefix. Substitute the exact envelope totals and
  transport the complete deterministic continuation back at cost $2\rho$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Complete honest-one and tampered-zero extraction]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-opposite-flipflop-true}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.flipFlopOpposite_true_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-opposite-flipflop,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-lookahead-refresh,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-fixed-tampering-refresh,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-factored-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor}
  Under the common size guard, take normalized original factors with
  left-source, honest-state, and right-source envelopes $\mu,\nu,\xi$,
  and original prefix error $\delta$. With $K,J,D,C,\varepsilon$ as
  above, set
  \[
    \rho=4\varepsilon+\delta+K(1+D^2)\sum\mu+
      KD^2\sum\nu+JC^3\sum\xi.
  \]
  The full actual honest-one/tampered-zero law has error at most
  \[
    2\rho+2\varepsilon+KD^5\sum\mu+JC^5\sum\xi,
  \]
  retaining both complete histories, the tampered final output, and the
  original left state. This completes the two opposite-bit orientations
  of Chattopadhyay--Goyal--Li Algorithm 1 and Lemma 6.8 for these programs.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound the honest first refresh while retaining the tampered refresh.
  Fix that tampered coordinate in the transcript and repair the honest
  coordinate. The next tampered seed is then left-only, so the fixed-side
  refresh theorem applies. Charge $2\rho$ to return to the actual law.
\end{proof}

  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-flipflop-transcript}

  \leanok

\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Preserving uniformity through a selected half-step]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-flipflop-preservation-half}

\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A complete weak step before advice differs]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-flipflop-weak}

  \leanok

  Under the common size guard and normalized original factors, suppose
  the honest state is $\rho$-close to uniform given $Z$. Let the
  nonnegative original $X/Y$ envelope totals be $\alpha,\beta$.
  For arbitrary current advice bits and correlated tampered state, the
  complete actual step, retaining both histories and the full left state
  but leaving the final tampered output unobserved, has error at most
  \[
    4\rho+12\varepsilon+K(1+D^2)(2+D^4)\alpha+
      3KD^2/C+J(2C^3+C^5)\beta.

  Only the incoming whole-state discrepancy and original source
  envelopes are assumed; the intermediate discrepancy is derived.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}

  factors and their original-source envelopes for the second pass.
\end{proof}

  discrepancy, either honest bit against its opposite has error at most

  \leanok
  In the zero branch, project the original whole-state guarantee through
  the balanced prefix map. In the one branch, repair the incoming state
  while preserving both original source envelopes, apply the capped-state
  result, and return to the actual retained law at cost $2\rho$.
\end{proof}

  The encoded runtime stores the sources, unary parameters, remaining
  advice, and current state explicitly.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Correct polynomial-time evaluation of the advice construction]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-program}

  flip-flop size guard it agrees with the vector construction. Every
  iteration, including those after advice exhaustion, has encoded length
  at most
  $4|x|+2|y|+2L+2e+2|\alpha|+2^{64}L+12$.

  \leanok
  Induct through the left-to-right fold using exact step correctness.
  Bound every component of the encoded state and apply bounded-iteration

  final extraction and total input decoder.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[An exact original-law invariant for an advice prefix]

  Once the advice prefixes differ, the transcript fixes the tampered

  This is internal proof data, not a security premise for the eventual

\begin{definition}[The conservative advice error recurrence]

  For $K,J,D,C,\varepsilon$ as above, put
  \[
    \Phi(\rho,\alpha,\beta)=4\rho+12\varepsilon+
      KD(1+D^2)(2+D^4)\alpha+3KD^2/C+J(2C^3+C^5)\beta.
  \]
  Set $\rho_0=\rho$ and
  $\rho_{i+1}=\Phi(\rho_i,D^{8i}\alpha,C^{5i}\beta)$.
  The common update conservatively covers all three step estimates.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The exact invariant through the actual advice fold]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-invariant}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.Internal.AdviceInvariant.step,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.Internal.AdviceInvariant.iterate}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-invariant,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-recurrence}
  Under the common size guard, an initial invariant extends through any
  list of paired advice bits. At length $a$, both current states equal
  their actual folds, the envelope totals are bounded by
  $D^{8a}\alpha,C^{5a}\beta$, and the whole-state error is at most
  $\rho_a$. The separated condition holds if it held initially or the
  two processed advice words differ. In that phase, the transcript fixes
  the actual tampered state. Every prefix retains exact factors of the
  original sample, including null conditional rows.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Before separation use the weak step. At the first unequal pair use
  opposite-advice extraction, then use full-step preservation thereafter.
  Appending the actual tampered state gives the next exact factors. Induct
  from the last paired bit and use the program's append identity.
  This follows the two-phase structure of Chattopadhyay--Goyal--Li,
  \href{https://arxiv.org/pdf/1505.00107}{Algorithm 2 and Lemma 6.9},
  with the displayed conservative error update.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Closed finite advice error and entropy reserves]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-error-bounds}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.Internal.adviceStepError_mono,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.Internal.adviceStepError_nonneg,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.Internal.adviceChainError_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.Internal.adviceChainError_final_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.Internal.adviceChainError_fixedReserve_dyadic_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-recurrence}
  The update is monotone and preserves nonnegative inputs. For
  $\rho_0=0$ and nonnegative $\alpha,\beta$,
  \[
    \rho_a\le a4^a\bigl[12\varepsilon+3KD^2/C+
      6KD^{8a+7}\alpha+3JC^{5a+5}\beta\bigr].
  \]
  Write $d=2^{24}L$ and $c=2^{64}L$. If
  $\alpha\le2^{-k_X}$, $\beta\le2^{-k_Y}$ and
  \[
    2^{62}L+2d+e\le c,\qquad
    2^{62}L+(8a+7)d+e\le k_X,\qquad
    2^{142}L+(5a+5)c+e\le k_Y,
  \]
  then $\rho_a+\varepsilon+KD^{8a+1}\alpha
    \le(24a4^a+2)2^{-e}$.
  Equal reserves $k_X=k_Y=2^{150}(a+1)L$ suffice when $e\le L$.
  Choosing $e=t+2a+\lceil\log_2(a+1)\rceil+10$ bounds this expression
  by $2^{-t}$. This is finite arithmetic for the recurrence and the
  extra final left extraction; it does not assume a program-security witness.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound the left and right coefficients by $6D^7$ and $3C^5$ and use
  the final-depth envelope powers throughout the factor-four recurrence.
  Each reserve term is at most $\varepsilon$. The logarithmic rounding
  in $e$ absorbs $24a4^a+2$, including the zero-length case.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Explicit total advice parameters]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceErrorExponent,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceScale,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceSourceEntropy}
  \leanok
  For left input length $n$, advice length $a$, target exponent $t$, and
  requested output $o$, set
  \[
    e=t+2a+\lceil\log_2(a+1)\rceil+10,\qquad
    L=1024\bigl(a+t+o+\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil+256\bigr),\qquad
    m=2^{150}(a+1)L.
  \]
  The total chooser uses $m$ both as source entropy reserve and intended
  right-source length. Capacity $m\le n$ remains an explicit condition.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite guards and reserves for the explicit chooser]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceParameters_sizeGuard,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceParameters_error_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceParameters_stateWidth_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceParameters_output_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceParameters_state_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceParameters_left_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceParameters_right_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceParameters_left_reserve_of_capacity}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-error-bounds}
  With $d=2^{24}L$ and $c=2^{64}L$, the chosen parameters satisfy the common program guard,
  $e\le L$, $c\le m$, $o\le d$, and all three displayed entropy
  reserves with $k_X=k_Y=m$. These facts hold for every natural input.
  When $m\le n$, the left reserve also fits in the original input width.
  Neither this capacity condition nor the required source entropy is
  asserted for arbitrary $n$. These constants and the conservative
  $4^a$ amplification are our finite deductions, not the sharper
  parameter bounds of the source construction.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound $\lceil\log_2(2^r(a+1)1024b+1)\rceil$ by $r+a+b+11$.
  The chosen scale then covers both source widths and the refreshed
  state. Apply the checked entropy reserve inequalities from $e\le L$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual retained law of the complete advice construction]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceCorrelationBreakerWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceCorrelationBreakerError}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-program,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-recurrence}
  Run both actual advice programs on the original factored sample
  $(Z,B,A)$. The retained law is $((Z,B,W'),W)$, where $W,W'$ are
  the honest and tampered outputs after the additional final extraction.
  Thus the whole original right state remains, including both right
  inputs when these are arbitrary functions of $(Z,B)$.
  For advice length $a$ and original left-envelope total $\alpha$, define
  \[
    \mathcal E(m,L,e,a,\alpha)=\varepsilon+
      a4^a\bigl[12\varepsilon+3KD^2/C+
        6KD^{8a+7}\alpha+3JC^{5a+5}2^{-m}\bigr]
      +KD^{8a+1}\alpha.
  \]
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Strong extraction by the complete actual advice chain]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceCorrelationBreaker_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-extraction,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-invariant,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-error-bounds,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage}
  Assume the common size guard, $c\le m$, normalized factors $w,l,r$,
  and nonnegative envelope $\mu$ satisfying
  $w(z)\Pr[x(z,A)=x_0\mid z]\le\mu(z)$.
  The honest $m$-bit right input is uniform on every conditional row.
  Honest and tampered inputs on each side are arbitrary functions of
  the same original state; the two sides are independent given $Z$.
  For two fixed unequal advice words of the same length $a$, the actual
  law $((Z,B,W'),W)$ is within
  $\mathcal E(m,L,e,a,\sum_z\mu(z))$ of its exact retained marginal
  with a fresh uniform honest output.
  No intermediate factorization, repaired seed, or security invariant is
  supplied as a theorem hypothesis.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The initial prefix of the uniform right input gives zero state error.
  Construct the initial invariant and iterate it through the actual paired
  advice. Unequal words force the separated phase. Apply the extra final
  left-source extraction while retaining the full original right state
  and actual tampered output, then substitute the finite recurrence bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[A finite dyadic guarantee for the complete advice chain]
  \label{cor:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-extraction-dyadic}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceCorrelationBreaker_dyadic_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-extraction,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-error-bounds}
  Under the preceding source, advice, and size hypotheses, set
  $e=t+2a+\lceil\log_2(a+1)\rceil+10$. If
  \[
    \sum_z\mu(z)\le2^{-2^{150}(a+1)L},\qquad
    2^{150}(a+1)L\le m,
  \]
  the actual complete program has retained-law error at most $2^{-t}$.
  The finite program guard uses this chosen local error exponent.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Conditional uniformity gives original right-envelope total $2^{-m}$.
  Apply the three checked reserves and the conservative dyadic error budget.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[A requested output prefix of the actual advice program]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-truncation}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceOutputPrefix,

    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceTruncatedCorrelationBreakerWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-extraction}
  Return the first $o$ coordinates of each actual output, completing
  positions beyond the full width $d$ by false. Retain the original
  $(Z,B)$ and the truncated tampered output. This is a total definition
  for all widths; the uniformity guarantee below concerns $o\le d$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact output truncation preserves the retained-law error]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-truncation}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceOutputPrefix_eq_of_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceOutputPrefix_self,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceOutputPrefix_apply_of_ge,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceOutputPrefix_uniform,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceTruncatedCorrelationBreakerWeight_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceTruncatedCorrelationBreakerWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceTruncatedCorrelationBreakerWeight_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-truncation}
  For $o\le d$, the prefix of a uniform full output is uniform on $o$
  bits. Simultaneously truncating honest and tampered outputs cannot
  increase the distance to the actual retained marginal times a uniform
  honest output. The raw finite-weight comparison requires no new source
  assumptions, and normalized input factors give a normalized truncated law.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Split uniform Boolean words into prefix and suffix coordinates. Apply
  deterministic contraction first to the honest output and then to the
  tampered coordinate in the retained tag.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[The actual requested output at the selected finite parameters]
  \label{cor:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-selected-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceTruncatedCorrelationBreaker_parameters_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-parameters,
    cor:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-extraction-dyadic,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-truncation}
  Choose $e,L,m$ from $n,a,t,o$ as above. Let the original law have
  normalized left and right factors given $Z$, with a nonnegative
  honest-left joint point-mass envelope $\mu$ of total at most $2^{-m}$.
  Suppose the honest right input is conditionally uniform on $m$ bits.
  For any two fixed unequal advice words of length $a$, the actual
  $o$-bit truncated program output has error at most $2^{-t}$, retaining
  the original $(Z,B)$ and the actual truncated tampered output.
  No size guard, reserve inequality, field instance, primitive extractor,
  or intermediate security witness is supplied by the caller.
  The source hypotheses govern feasibility; the theorem does not assert
  the reserve fits every left-input width. The selected runtime is
  certified below; later asymptotic family composition remains separate.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Discharge the dyadic theorem's program guards, state width, and entropy
  reserves with the total chooser. Apply the exact prefix contraction at
  the chooser's proved output-width bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[One total selected-parameter advice evaluator]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-selected-runtime}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.adviceSelectedRightWord,

  with false completion, then execute the actual advice program and

\end{definition}

  \leanok

  Apply the bounded complete advice runtime and the output-prefix

  and semantic program as the selected statistical theorem.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The actual advice breaker on a nearly uniform right input]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-perturbed}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_factored_uniform_right_repair_rows,

  Keep the original normalized factored source, left envelope, size
  guards, and unequal equal-length advice hypotheses. Replace exact
  right-input uniformity by distance at most $\rho$ from uniform
  jointly with $Z$. The actual complete program has error at most
  $\rho+\mathcal E(a,\sum_z\mu(z))$, and at most $\rho+2^{-t}$
  under the dyadic reserves. It retains the original $Z$, full right
  state, and actual tampered final output.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Construct a uniform right-coordinate repair preserving the original
  right marginal on every row, including null transcript rows. The
  original left kernel and tampered program are unchanged. Project
  away the repaired coordinate after applying the exact-uniform
  theorem. The actual retained marginal is equal before and after
  repair, so the transfer charges $\rho$ only once.
\end{proof}

  left state, and actual tampered output.

\begin{theorem}[Source/seed reversal with an observed message]
