\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual first phase of the affine conversion]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.AffinePhaseOneCopies,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneFirstSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneFirstOutput,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSecondSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneOutput,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneRightMessage,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneTranscript,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneTranscriptWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneTranscriptLeft,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneTranscriptRight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneTranscriptLeftEnvelope,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneTranscriptRightEnvelope}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-advice-program,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  Follow the three-stage first phase of Chattopadhyay--Liao,
  Theorem~6.1, \url{https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.12652}.
  On the same masked source $X=A+B$, first extract linearly with a
  prefix of the right input, run the actual advice breaker with the
  source/seed roles reversed, and extract linearly again using its output.
  Index the honest execution by $\mathsf{none}$ and the $t$ tampered
  executions by $\mathsf{some}(i)$.
  The transcript records all initial prefixes and mask contributions,
  all first extraction outputs, then all advice outputs. It retains
  the original latent variables in its factored representation.
  These total definitions alone assert no statistical guarantee.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The actual first extracted seed in the affine phase]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-initial}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneFirstSeed_uniform,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOne_initial_seed_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-affine,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-laws}
  Let the original law have normalized factors given $Z$, a
  nonnegative honest-left joint envelope $\mu$, and a conditionally
  uniform honest right word of length $d$. Assume
  \[
    \lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L_0,\quad 64\le L_0,\quad
    e_0+66\le L_0,\quad 2^{24}L_0\le d.
  \]
  The honest seed prefix is uniform. The actual first masked
  extraction, retaining $Z$ and every honest/tampered initial prefix
  and mask contribution, has distance from uniform at most
  \[
    2^{-e_0}+2^{\,2^{142}L_0}\sum_z\mu(z).
  \]
  No intermediate seed certificate is a premise.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A sufficiently long uniform word has a balanced prefix. Apply the
  actual depth-64 affine extraction theorem while retaining the
  entire original right state. Project this state onto the complete
  first right message and use the exact fixed-seed XOR identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact first-phase factors and envelope accounting]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-transcript}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneFirstOutput_xor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneFirstLeft_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSecondRight_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneOutput_eq_xor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneRight_probability,

    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneTranscript_right_envelope,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneRightLeftEnvelope_sum,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneLeftEnvelope_sum,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneRightEnvelope_sum,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneTranscriptLeftEnvelope_sum,

  \leanok

    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript,

    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor}
  For normalized original factors, each of the three observations
  has an exact factorization retaining the original left and right
  states. All conditional kernels are normalized, including null
  rows. The actual first and final extraction outputs are the XOR
  of their corresponding side-local contributions.
  Write $d_0=2^{24}L_0$, $r_0=2^{64}L_0$, and $d_1=2^{24}L_1$.
  Nonnegative original source envelopes $\mu,\xi$ become envelopes
  with exact totals
  \[
    2^{(t+1)r_0}\sum_z\mu(z),\qquad
    2^{(t+1)(d_0+r_0+d_1)}\sum_z\xi(z),
  \]
  respectively. These exact execution and counting identities feed
  the complete pairwise first-phase guarantee below; the subsequent
  subset-doubling construction remains separate.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Alternate the existing right-, left-, and right-observation
  identities on the original factors. Opposite-side observations
  preserve envelope totals; own-side observations copy the envelope
  across the message alphabet. There are $(2^w)^{t+1}$ families
  of $w$-bit words, and the first right message has alphabet size
  $(2^{d_0+r_0})^{t+1}$. The matched extractor's exact XOR law
  identifies every side-local formula with the actual program.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual pair laws and full first-phase error]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-pair}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSecondSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneLeftPairWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOnePairWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneError}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one}
  For one tampered index $i$, the second-seed law retains the first
  right message, complete original left state, and tampered advice output.
  The final pair laws retain the entire executed transcript, original
  right state, and corresponding tampered final value. One law contains
  the original-left extraction contributions; the other contains the
  actual masked program outputs. Set
  $r_0=2^{64}L_0$, $r=2^hL_1$, $F=2^{2h+14}L_1$, and $M=\sum\mu$.
  Their complete error budget is
  \[
    2^{-e_r}+2^{-e_0}+2^{-e_1^*}
      +2^{2^{142}L_0}M+2^{F+r+(t+1)r_0}M,
  \]
  where $e_1^*$ is the requested error exponent of the advice call,
  whose internal exponent is selected by its checked error schedule.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The actual advice-generated second seed]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-second}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSecondSeedWeight_eq_alternating,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSecondSeedWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOne_second_seed_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-pair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-initial,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-advice}
  Suppose the original factors are normalized, the honest right word
  is conditionally uniform, and a nonnegative original-left envelope
  has total $M$. For one tampering with unequal advice of the same
  length, assume the finite advice guards and original-right entropy
  reserve after all first right messages. The actual second-seed law
  has error at most $2^{-e_0}+2^{2^{142}L_0}M+2^{-e_1^*}$.
  No intermediate seed certificate is supplied.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Identify the actual advice call after the right observation, apply
  the original-input first-seed bound, and use the perturbed-seed
  advice theorem with the reversed source roles. The observation's
  full alphabet cost is discharged by the explicit source reserve.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Final pairwise merging in the first phase]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-final}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOnePair_dist_le_left,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneLeftPairWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOnePairWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneLeftPair_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOnePair_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-pair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-transcript,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-growing,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage}
  Under the growing-depth budget, if the actual second-seed pair has
  error $\delta$, both final pair laws have error at most
  $2^{-e_r}+\delta+2^{F+r+(t+1)r_0}M$.
  The estimate retains the complete executed transcript and original
  right state, as well as the indicated tampered final value.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The original-left envelope pays for the first-output family.
  Two-sided merging pays for one tampered final output. Source linearity
  identifies the masked output with a right-state-dependent XOR
  translation, preserving the corresponding uniform target exactly.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Complete pairwise first phase from original inputs]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOne_left_pair_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOne_pair_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-second,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-final}
  From the original normalized factors, a conditionally uniform honest
  right input, original-left envelope, fixed unequal equal-length advice,
  and the displayed finite component guards and reserves, both actual
  final pair laws satisfy the complete error budget above.
  This proves the pairwise first phase of Chattopadhyay--Liao
  Theorem~6.1, printed p.23. Joint security against larger tampering
  subsets still requires the later doubling rounds.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Compose the proved first-seed, advice-generated second-seed, and
  final extraction estimates. Rewrite the exact alphabet sizes as
  binary exponents. Every security premise concerns the original source.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite dyadic reserves for the complete first phase]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-dyadic}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneError_dyadic_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-pair}
  Set all three local error targets to $e+3$. If $M\le2^{-k}$ and
  \[
    2^{142}L_0+e+3\le k,\qquad
    F+r+(t+1)r_0+e+3\le k,
  \]
  then the complete first-phase error is at most $2^{-e}$.
  These are conservative finite sufficient reserves; a nonvacuous
  application still needs an original source meeting the mass bound.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Each of the three local errors and two source-mass contributions
  is at most $2^{-(e+3)}$. Their sum is bounded by $2^{-e}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[An explicit chooser for the complete first phase]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneBase,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneScale,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneInitialScale,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneRightBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneLocalError,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSourceEntropy}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-pair,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-growing-runtime}
  For source width $n$, tampering count $t$, advice length $a$, and
  target error exponent $s$, set
  \[
    B=a+s+\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil+\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil+256,
  \]
  \[
    L_1=2^{20}B^2,\quad L_0=2^{90}(a+1)L_1,\quad
    d=2^{160}(t+1)(a+1)BL_1.
  \]
  Use $h=\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil+64$, local target $e=s+3$, and
  \[
    k=2^{142}L_0+2^{2h+14}L_1+2^hL_1+(t+1)2^{64}L_0+e.
  \]
  The advice call uses its checked internal error schedule for target
  $e$. These explicit conservative choices do not assert the paper's
  sharp constants or universal capacity $k\le n$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Checked finite guards and uniform parameter generation]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_right_log,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_scale_log,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_initial_length,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_initial_room,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_initial_error,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_growing_guard,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_advice_guard,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_output_size,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_seed_width,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_source_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_prefix_size,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_initial_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_final_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneParameters_entropy_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneBase_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneScale_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneInitialScale_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneRightBits_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneLocalError_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSourceEntropy_unaryFn}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-dyadic,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-growing-runtime}
  Every numerical guard of the initial extraction, advice call, and
  final extraction holds for the chosen parameters. The right width
  contains the initial seed prefix and pays the complete first
  right-message family plus advice reserve. The chosen left entropy
  pays both dyadic source charges. All six chooser values have total
  uniform polynomial-time unary generators, without a source-capacity
  or guard promise.
  The finite bound $k\le2^{256}(t+1)^2(a+1)B^2$ can be used to
  discharge source capacity in concrete applications.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound the logarithms of the polynomially enlarged widths in terms
  of $B$, then use the fixed scale factors to discharge the guards.
  The two left reserves are summands of $k$. The logarithmic depth has
  polynomially bounded powers, so registered unary arithmetic computes
  every chosen value on all inputs.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The actual pairwise guarantee at the chosen parameters]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-selected}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOne_left_pair_parameters_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOne_pair_parameters_dist_le}

    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-dyadic,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-parameters}
  Use the named chooser at $(n,t,a,s)$. Suppose the original law has
  normalized factors given $Z$, the honest right word is conditionally
  uniform at the selected width $d$, and the original-left envelope
  has total at most $2^{-k}$. For any selected tampered index whose
  fixed advice and the honest advice both have length $a$ and differ,
  both actual final pair laws have distance at most $2^{-s}$ from
  their actual retained marginal times uniform output.
  No numerical guard, intermediate seed estimate, or repaired source
  is a premise. The original source still must satisfy the chosen
  entropy bound; capacity is not claimed for every $n$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Instantiate the complete original-input estimate with every proved
  chooser guard. The two explicit source reserves and three local
  target exponents give the finite dyadic error bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The complete three-call first-phase string program]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneFirstSeedWord,

  False-complete the first seed prefix, perform the first depth-64
  extraction, run the actual advice program with reversed input roles,
  and feed its output to the growing-depth final extraction on the
  same supplied source word. The paired evaluator reads three data words and
  six unary numerical words. Each component keeps its total behavior
  on invalid inputs.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact uniform polynomial-time first-phase evaluation]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneFirstSeedWord_length,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneFirstSeed_ofFn,

  The runtime and its single paired evaluator belong uniformly to
  $\mathrm{FP}$ on every string. Under the three component guards,
  canonical words compute exactly the semantic first-phase output at
  $h=\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil+64$, with no statistical premise.
  The paired output length is at most $2^{65}(|z|+1)^2$.
  Its exact output width is $2^hL_1$ when the final growing guard
  holds, and zero otherwise.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Compose the three registered runtime certificates and exact output
  equations. Both supplied input words remain available throughout.
  Prefix padding agrees with the semantic seed even for short words,
  and the growing final call supplies the unconditional output bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The selected first-phase evaluator]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-selected-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSelectedRuntime,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affinePhaseOneSelectedEval}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-program,

  With unary $t,s$, compute the complete chooser, truncate or

  and advice words followed by the two unary parameter words.
  No source-entropy or capacity test is performed.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[One total polynomial-time selected first phase]

  The single paired evaluator belongs to $\mathrm{FP}$ on every
  encoded string, including malformed data and infeasible entropy

  semantic first phase, with no numerical guard or source premise.
  Statistical security remains the separate original-source theorem

\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok

  and full runtime certificate. The finite chooser discharges each

\begin{theorem}[Conditional uniformity under actual observations]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-transcript-distance}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_uniformSecond_map_output_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedSeedWeight_image_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedSeedWeight_transform_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.observedSeedWeight_observe_other_dist}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  Balanced output projections and transcript-dependent output bijections
  preserve or contract distance from the actual retained marginal times
  uniform. In an original factored law, observing an independent opposite
  side leaves the averaged discrepancy exactly unchanged. The equality
  requires nonnegative transcript weights and normalized opposite kernels;
  it includes zero-mass observations.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Push forward both the actual law and its conditional-uniform comparison.
  For the opposite-side observation, sum its normalized message law on
  each original transcript row.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Alternating extraction with selected tampered outputs]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-copies}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.alternatingCopiesWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-laws}
  Read all left seeds and right source copies from the original factored
  sample. Apply the same extractor to each pair, retaining the original
  transcript, every executed left seed, and the tampered outputs indexed
  by $S$, alongside the honest output.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[An actual alternating call retains the selected copies]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-copies}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.alternatingCopiesWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.alternatingCopiesWeight_eq_observed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.alternating_copies_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-copies,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-sided-leakage}
  For normalized original factors, a threshold-$K$ strong extractor of
  nonnegative error $\varepsilon$ and nonempty seed/output alphabets,
  suppose the honest left seed has error $\delta$ against its copies in
  $S$. If a nonnegative joint envelope $\mu(z)$ bounds the original
  honest right source, the actual call has error at most
  $\varepsilon+\delta+K|\mathrm{Out}|^{|S|}\sum_z\mu(z)$.
  The law is exactly the observed seed law after all executed left seeds
  are fixed, so it supplies the next alternating call.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Regard the selected tampered outputs as left-dependent finite leakage
  after swapping the two original factors. Sum over the original opposite
  state to identify the next observed law.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Smooth independence merging on overlapping subsets]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-independence}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothIndependenceSourceWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothIndependenceSeedWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothIndependenceMergingWeight}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-merging}
  Let the original left state determine $X,X_1,\ldots,X_t$ and an actual
  prefix $Q$. The right state determines its seed family after $(Z,Q)$ is
  fixed. The source comparison retains $(Z,X_T)$; the seed comparison
  retains $(Z,Q,Y_S)$. The extraction law is the direct original-law image
  retaining $(Z,Q)$, complete original right state $B$, and all actual
  tampered outputs in $S\cup T$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Actual smooth independence merging]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-independence}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothIndependenceSourceWeight_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothIndependenceSeedWeight_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.smoothIndependenceMergingWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.smooth_independence_merging_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-independence,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-merging}
  With normalized original factors and a threshold-$K$ strong extractor
  of nonnegative error $\varepsilon$ and nonempty seed/output alphabets,
  source discrepancy $\rho$ and actual prefix-conditioned seed discrepancy
  $\delta$ imply error at most
  \[
    \varepsilon+\delta+\rho+
      K|\mathrm{Out}|^{|S|}|Q|/|X|.
  \]
  The subsets may overlap. No conditional point-mass cap or repaired-source
  witness is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Use $X_T$ as the original left observation and the outputs on $S$ as
  leakage in smooth merging. Retain the stored $S$ output on overlaps;
  reconstruct the remaining $T$ outputs from $X_T$ and the full right
  state. This deterministic projection introduces no additional error.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual four-call affine round and its transcript]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundPrefix,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundMiddleSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundMiddleOutput,

  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-extractor,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-observed-transcript}
  Starting with a long row, use its short prefix to extract from the
  original right word, use that seed to extract a short row, reread the
  original right word with this short row, then extract the next long row
  from the unchanged original affine input $X=A\mathbin{\oplus}B$.
  The proof tracks its left contribution and right mask separately.
  All calls are actual matched extractors.
  The transcript observes right prefix masks, actual prefixes, the first
  right seeds and short-row mask contributions, actual short rows, then
  the second right seeds. This is the round in Chattopadhyay--Liao,
  Theorem~6.1, printed pp.23--25.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Exact factorization of the executed affine round]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-factors}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundPrefixLeftMessage_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundMiddleSeedRight_eq,

  For normalized original factors, each stage is exactly the original
  joint law with its actual observations appended. Both original latent
  states survive in the conditional kernels, which are normalized even
  on null rows. Linearity identifies the short and final actual rows
  with the XOR of their original-left and original-right contributions.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Alternate deterministic right and left observations. False-completed
  prefixes preserve XOR, as do the fixed-seed matched extractors.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Source envelopes survive the complete affine round]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-envelopes}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundMiddle_right_envelope,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundTranscript_left_envelope,

  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-factors}
  If $C$ is the cardinality of the entire honest/tampered short-message
  family, original nonnegative joint source envelopes with totals
  $\alpha,\beta$ acquire final totals $C^2\alpha,C^4\beta$.
  Before the final right extraction its envelope total is $C^3\beta$.
  Opposite-side observations preserve totals exactly; observations of
  the source side pay the corresponding finite message alphabet.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply the exact conditional-kernel point-mass identities at every
  observation. Sum normalized opposite messages, including zero-mass rows.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Actual intermediate and final retained-subset laws]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-laws}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundLeftRowsWeight,

  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round}
  Each law reads only actual original inputs and executed calls. The
  final left and masked output laws retain the complete transcript,
  original right state, and selected tampered outputs. Intermediate
  seed laws are the exact conditional marginals needed by the next call;
  no security property or replacement-source witness is a definition.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The four actual calls compose through retained seed laws]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-stages}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundPrefix_uniform,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundPrefixSeed_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundMiddleSeed_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRound_middle_left_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRound_middle_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundMiddleSeedInputWeight_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRound_middle_seed_input_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundFinalSeed_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundLeftSubset_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundSubset_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundNextLeftRows_eq_map,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundNextLeftRows_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-laws,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-transcript-distance,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-alternating-copies,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-smooth-independence,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-envelopes,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-growing}
  Under the relevant finite matched-extractor guards, the old-row
  discrepancy controls the actual first right seed. Smooth merging
  combines the two selected subsets in the actual short row. The second
  right extraction uses that exact seed-input law and the original-right
  envelope. The last call rereads the original affine input; its left
  contribution is bounded using the original-left envelope. Masking with
  retained right contributions and taking the next-left marginal add no
  error. These individual implications compose in the theorem below.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Observe right prefix masks without changing the averaged old-left
  discrepancy, then use balanced prefix transport and alternating
  extraction. Apply smooth independence merging to the original actual
  prefix, append the complete right messages, and use fibrewise XOR.
  Recover a final seed by the next alternating call and finish with
  source-envelope extraction, retaining the executed transcript throughout.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Exact finite error of one subset-union round]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-error}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundError}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-laws}
  Put $c=2^{24}L$, $m=2^hL$, $k_{24}=2^{62}L$, and
  $k_h=2^{2h+14}L$. For $s=|S|$, $u=|S\cup T|$ and original source
  envelope totals $\alpha,\beta$, define
  \begin{align*}
    \mathcal E={}&4\cdot2^{-e}+\rho_S+\rho_T
      +2^{k_{24}+(s+t+1)c}\beta
      +\frac{2^{k_{24}+(s+t+1)c}}{2^m}\\
      &+2^{k_{24}+(u+3(t+1))c}\beta
      +2^{k_h+um+2(t+1)c}\alpha.
  \end{align*}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[One actual round merges two subset guarantees]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRound_left_union_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRound_union_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRound_next_union_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-stages,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-error}
  Let the original sources have normalized factors given $Z$ and
  nonnegative joint point-mass envelopes of totals $\alpha,\beta$.
  Assume $c\le m$, the three input log-lengths are at most $L$, $64\le L$,
  $e+26\le L$, $e+h+2\le L$, and
  \[
    (h+1)(e+2h+\lceil\log_2(L+1)\rceil+4)\le L.
  \]
  If the old honest left row has discrepancies $\rho_S,\rho_T$ against
  the respective tampered subsets, the actual next output has error at
  most $\mathcal E$, retaining the complete executed transcript, full
  original right state, and all actual outputs in $S\cup T$.
  The same bound holds for original-left contributions and for the new
  conditional left-row law. The sets may overlap. No intermediate seed,
  repaired source, primitive extractor, or security witness is supplied.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Compose all four preceding actual-law implications and substitute the
  exact original-source envelope totals. Deterministic marginal projection
  exposes the next left-row invariant.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Four finite reserves give a dyadic round bound]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-dyadic}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundError_dyadic_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-error}
  If $\alpha\le2^{-k_x}$, $\beta\le2^{-k_y}$ and
  \begin{align*}
    k_{24}+(s+t+1)c+e&\le k_y, &
    k_{24}+(s+t+1)c+e&\le m,\\
    k_{24}+(u+3(t+1))c+e&\le k_y, &
    k_h+um+2(t+1)c+e&\le k_x,
  \end{align*}
  then $\mathcal E\le\rho_S+\rho_T+8\cdot2^{-e}$.
  The reserve assumptions are explicit finite inequalities, not an
  asserted global iteration schedule.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Each source or old-row charge is at most one local error. Add these
  four charges to the four actual extractor errors.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite subsets split at twice the capacity]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-subset}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_union_of_card_le_two_mul}
  \leanok
  If $|U|\le2k$, there exist disjoint $S,T\subseteq U$ with
  $S\cup T=U$ and $|S|,|T|\le k$, including $k=0$.
  This supplies the finite combinatorial split for a doubling update;
  it does not itself iterate the probabilistic invariant.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Choose a subset of size $\min(k,|U|)$ and take its complement in $U$.
\end{proof}

    \operatorname{pair}(t_{\rm word},
      \operatorname{pair}(L_{\rm word},e_{\rm word})))$.
  The three parameter-word lengths specify $t,L,e$. False-complete the
  old row's short prefix and execute the four component string programs,
  rereading the original source words. The final depth is
  $h=\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil+64$.
\end{definition}

  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-program}

\begin{theorem}[The first phase initializes every empty or singleton subset]

\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok

\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Split every requested set of size at most $2k$ into two sets of size
  at most $k$. Apply the actual round union bound and the uniform finite
  reserves, then identify the next conditional left-row law.
\end{proof}

  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-iteration-factors}

\begin{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Actual initialization connects the complete program to its factored law]

  round iteration. Pointwise XOR identities identify every retained

\begin{theorem}[The invariant follows every actual round and bounds masked outputs]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-iteration-invariant}

  after $i$ rounds has capacity $2^ik$ and error
  $\rho_i=2^i\rho_0+8(2^i-1)2^{-e}$.
  The same error bounds the actual XOR outputs while retaining the
  complete executed transcript, the original right state, and any subset
  of at most $2^ik$ tampered outputs.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct with the doubling theorem, obtaining each round's envelopes
  from the original ones and the exact mass identities. Append the
  conditionally independent right state without changing the discrepancy.
  The honest right mask is a fiberwise XOR bijection, and masking the
  retained tampered outputs only processes the retained coordinates.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[A finite global schedule for the affine iteration]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-iteration-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationRounds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationTarget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationSourceEntropy,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationInitialLeftLoss,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationInitialRightLoss,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationMessageBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationLeftEntropy,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationRightEntropy}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-parameters}
  Set $R=\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil$ and
  $\sigma=\mathrm{target}+R+1$. Evaluate the unchanged first-phase chooser
  at $\sigma$, writing its widths as $L_0,L,D$, its original-left entropy
  reserve as $\kappa$, and its local error exponent as $e=\sigma+3$.
  The original-left reserve for the complete construction is $2\kappa$.
  Write $m_0=2^{64}L_0$, $c_0=2^{24}L_0$, and $c=2^{24}L$.
  The initial transcript losses are $A_x=(t+1)m_0$ and
  $A_y=(t+1)(c_0+m_0+c)$; one short-message family costs $M=(t+1)c$ bits.
  The remaining source entropies before round $i$ are
  $2\kappa-A_x-2iM$ and $D-A_y-4iM$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The global schedule pays every actual round and the final error]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-iteration-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationRounds_cover,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_left_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_right_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_first_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_merge_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_recover_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_final_reserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_left_mass,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_right_mass,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_error_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineIterationParameters_entropy_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-iteration-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-round-dyadic}
  The remaining entropies are nonnegative through all $R$ rounds. At
  every $i<R$ they satisfy all four worst-case reserves from the actual
  doubling theorem. Exact dyadic identities give the propagated masses
  after the initial transcript and all prior round messages.
  The error recurrence satisfies
  \[
    2^R2^{-\sigma}+8(2^R-1)2^{-e}\le2^{-\mathrm{target}}.
  \]
  Moreover, if $B$ is the first-phase chooser base at $\sigma$, then
  $2\kappa\le2^{257}(t+1)^2(a+1)B^2$. These finite bounds do not assume
  that every input length can support this entropy.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The sixty-four spare depth levels pay the retained long outputs and
  repeated short messages. The original right width has an additional
  base factor paying its complete observation budget. Subtract only
  earlier observations to obtain each current round's reserves. The
  doubled original-left budget also pays initialization. Expanding the
  geometric error recurrence and using $e=\sigma+3$ gives the final bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The complete actual affine construction and its string evaluator]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-construction}

  construction runs $R=\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil$ rounds and uses the

  Its retained law is the direct image of the original factored source
  law, keeping the original tag, full right state, and selected tampered
  outputs alongside the honest output.
  The selected string evaluator computes these parameters and normalizes
  the original right word before running the construction.

\begin{theorem}[One uniform program computes the complete affine construction]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-construction-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineRoundsStepEval_iterate,

  A unary number of actual rounds has one total uniform $\mathrm{FP}$
  evaluator. The selected complete evaluator agrees exactly with the

  Its output length is $2^hL$, where $h=\lceil\log_2(t+1)\rceil+64$ and
  $L$ is the selected scale. Polynomial time holds on all strings and

  with the actual first phase and unary parameter computation; canonical
  agreement follows by induction using the one-round execution identity.
