\begin{theorem}[The complete actual affine construction breaks all tampering correlations]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-construction-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineCorrelationBreakerWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineCorrelationBreaker_parameters_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-construction,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-phase-one-subsets,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-iteration-initial,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-iteration-invariant,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-iteration-parameters}
  Let $t>0$ and use the selected finite parameters. Suppose the original
  tag and two conditional factors are normalized, the honest right word
  is uniform at every original tag, and the original left-word joint
  masses have a nonnegative envelope $\mu$ with
  $\sum_z\mu(z)\le2^{-2\kappa}$. Let all advice words have length $a$,
  with every tampered advice different from the honest advice.
  Then the actual honest output is within $2^{-\mathrm{target}}$ of
  uniform while retaining the original tag, the full original right
  state, and every actual tampered output. The same bound holds for
  every subset of the tamperings. All calls use the same original affine
  input, and the theorem requires no intermediate seed, row-invariant,
  or numerical-guard hypothesis.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The actual first phase initializes capacity one at error $2^{-\sigma}$.
  Its exact transcript envelopes and the chosen finite reserves supply
  the actual induction through $R$ rounds. Since $t\le2^R$, its final
  invariant covers every tampering set, and the chosen error recurrence
  is at most $2^{-\mathrm{target}}$. Append the original right state,
  apply the known XOR masks, identify the complete executed program,
  and discard only the intermediate transcript by contraction.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Actual affine outputs with deterministic seed leakage]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-leakage}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakageOutputWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakageBadSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakageSourceEntropy}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-construction}
  Write $F$ for the complete selected affine construction, with seed width
  $d$, source reserve $k$, and error $2^{-q}$. Fix advice
  $\alpha_0,\ldots,\alpha_t$ and deterministic source observations
  $L_0,\ldots,L_t$ of width $d$.
  The actual leaky law retains all
  $F(A+b,y_i\oplus L_i(A),\alpha_i)$ for $i>0$, together with the honest
  output $F(A+b,y\oplus L_0(A),\alpha_0)$.
  An honest seed is bad at threshold $\gamma$ if some shift and tampered
  seed tuple give conditional-uniformity distance greater than $\gamma$.
  The complete leakage reserve is $k+(t+1)d$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The actual affine construction has few leaky bad seeds]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-leakage}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakageOutputWeight_probability,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakageOutputWeight_average_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakageBadSeeds_parameters_card_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakageBadSeeds_card_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.not_mem_affineLeakageBadSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakageSourceEntropy_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-construction-extraction}
  Suppose $t>0$, $\gamma>0$, the original source is normalized with point
  masses at most $2^{-(k+(t+1)d)}$, and all advice has length $a$, with
  the honest advice distinct from every tampered advice. For every shift
  and tampered-seed tuple chosen as functions of the honest base seed,
  the average actual leaky-output distance is at most $2^{-q}$.
  Consequently the bad-seed fraction is at most $2^{-q}/\gamma$.
  The full leakage reserve is at most
  $2^{258}(t+1)^2(a+1)B^3$, for the boosted common parameter base $B$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Following Chattopadhyay--Liao, Lemma~5.3, reveal the complete tuple of
  leakage observations. The conditional source kernel and the uniform
  base seed give an exact factorization, including zero-mass transcripts.
  Its joint source envelope sums to at most $2^{-k}$. XOR with each fixed
  honest leakage word preserves uniformity, so the actual affine theorem
  applies. Forgetting the leakage tag gives the exact base-seed average.
  Choose adversarial witnesses separately at each bad seed and bound
  their number by the nonnegative average.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Actual linear-sampler calls have small parity bias]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-leakage-parity}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightSign_mul_le_dist,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightSign_xor_le_dist,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.mapWeight_uniform_bool_coordinate,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakage_sign_mul_le_of_not_mem_badSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakage_coordinate_sign_le_of_not_mem_badSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakage_linear_parity_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineLeakage_linear_parameters}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-leakage}
  Let the selected sampler maps be linear in the original source.
  Fix a second summand $b$ and any coordinate of the affine output.
  If the honest sampler value at $b$ escapes the actual bad set, the
  absolute sign bias of the complete selected component parity is at
  most $2\gamma$. The actual selected construction supplies both this
  estimate and the preceding bad-set cardinality bound from the
  original source hypotheses alone.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  A coordinate of a uniform Boolean word is uniform. Its sign has zero
  mean even after multiplication by any bounded statistic of the
  retained tampered outputs. Twice total variation bounds the remaining
  expectation. Use the product of tampered signs as that statistic.
  Source linearity identifies all leaky calls with their actual sampler
  calls, as in Chattopadhyay--Liao, Lemma~5.4, equation~(5).
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The deterministic XOR source reduction]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-source-reduction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineSourceReduction}
  \leanok
  At each outer coordinate, XOR all component bits obtained by applying
  the correlation breaker to the same original source, the sampler's
  candidate seed, and the candidate advice. This is a deterministic map;
  statistical and runtime guarantees are separate statements.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Grouping component bits preserves absolute parity bias]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-source-reduction-parity}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineSourceReduction_parity_bias_eq}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-source-reduction}
  For every finite outer-coordinate set and every weighted input law,
  the absolute bias of the reduction's sign product equals that of the
  full component-call sign product. This includes zero candidates and
  the empty coordinate set.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  With true represented by $+1$, XOR of $C$ bits contributes the
  product of their signs times $(-1)^{C+1}$. The resulting constant
  factor has absolute value one for every outer parity.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual amplified linear sampler]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-amplified-matched-sampler}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSampler,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSamplerEntropy,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSamplerRuntime,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSamplerEval,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionIndexEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionAdvice}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-padded,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-growing-runtime}
  Set $b=2^{24}L$ and $h=\lceil\log_2(d+1)\rceil+64$.
  For an outer word of width $8b$ and candidate word of width
  $s=\mathrm{gammaBlockSeedBudget}(b)$, use the actual padded Gamma
  output as the seed of the actual growing matched extractor at error
  $1/16$. Read its first $d$ bits, completing with false bits if needed.
  Little-endian binary codes enumerate the outer and candidate words;
  concatenating the codes gives injective advice of length $8b+s$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Sampling and uniform evaluation of the actual composition]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-amplified-matched-sampler}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSampler_somewhereSampler,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSampler_xor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSampler_output_capacity,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSamplerRuntime_length,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSamplerRuntime_eq_amplifiedMatchedSampler,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSamplerRuntime_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.amplifiedMatchedSamplerEval_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.SomewhereSampler.reindex}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-amplified-matched-sampler,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-matched-growing,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-padded,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-sampler-amplification}
  Under $L>0$ and the growing-matched and Gamma numerical guards, the
  actual composition is a somewhere sampler with source threshold
  $2^{2^{2h+14}L+1}$, test allowance $2^{-5b}$, and failure $1/2$.
  At every fixed pair of indices it preserves source XOR, without any
  parameter guard. Its single total evaluator belongs to $\mathrm{FP}$
  and returns exactly $d$ bits on every input. Under the growing guard
  it agrees with the semantic sampler; binary reindexing preserves the
  sampling guarantee.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Truncate the growing matched extractor, convert it to a sampler using
  one extra source-entropy bit, and apply Gamma's $3/4$ neighbor coverage.
  The exact amplification budget is
  $2^{2b}\le 2\cdot2^{-5b}\cdot2^{8b}$. Compose the two actual runtimes,
  truncate and complete their output, and use their uniform certificates.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One fixed affine breaker supplies every small parity test]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-fixed-affine-tests}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.affineSourceReduction_exists_tests}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-leakage-parity,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-source-reduction-parity}
  Fix $C>0$, advice length $a>0$, target exponent $q$, and
  $\gamma>0$. Use the same actual affine breaker with $4C-1$
  tamperings for every parity test. For a normalized original source
  satisfying its complete leakage cap, any source-linear sampler and
  injective length-$a$ advice give bad-seed sets of relative size at
  most $2^{-q}/\gamma$. Every nonempty parity on at most four outer
  coordinates has a distinguished coordinate, chosen before the
  candidate and second summand. If that candidate seed escapes the
  corresponding bad set, the actual XOR-reduction parity has absolute
  sign expectation at most $2\gamma$ at every second-source fixing.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Embed all real calls except the honest call into the $4C-1$ slots.
  Fill unused slots with zero leakage and same-length advice obtained
  by flipping the honest advice's first bit. Apply the actual leakage
  theorem with the product of only the real tampered signs as its
  bounded mask. Linearity and the XOR grouping identity identify the
  original reduction. No unused output enters its parity.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual finite sumset extractor]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-finite-sumset-construction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionSeedBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionEntropy,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionSampler,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionOutput,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-amplified-matched-sampler,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-affine-source-reduction}
  Set $N=2^{8b}$, $C=2^s$, $a=8b+s$, $q=54b+s+10$, and
  $\gamma=2^{-(17b+10)}$. Let $d$ be the selected seed width of the
  fixed affine breaker with $4C-1$ tamperings. Use the actual enumerated
  sampler at width $d$ and the binary advice above, XOR the $C$ calls
  at each of the $N$ outer coordinates, and take their strict majority.
  The source entropy $k$ is the maximum of the actual affine leakage
  reserve and the amplified sampler reserve.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The actual finite construction extracts]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-finite-sumset-construction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionExtractor_flat,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReduction_union_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReduction_moment_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReduction_majority_guards}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-finite-sumset-construction,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-amplified-matched-sampler,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-fixed-affine-tests,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-parity-selection,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-extractor-parity-target}
  For $L>0$ and the two numerical sampler guards, the actual finite
  construction is a flat-source sumset extractor with threshold $2^k$
  and error $35/72$. No extractor, sampler, affine-security, or parity
  estimate is a hypothesis.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The exact identity $N^4 C\,2^{-q}/\gamma=2^{-5b}$ pays for all
  small parity tests. Apply the actual sampler and select good
  coordinates on at least half the second-source fixings. The finite
  budgets give $100m^2(2\gamma)\le1$, $m>0$, and
  $N-m\le\sqrt m/8$; here $L>0$ implies $b\ge5$.
  Apply the checked moment and majority theorem to these actual parities.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The actual family has eventual guards and sublinear entropy]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-sumset-construction-asymptotics}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionCandidateCount_isLittleO,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_sourceReductionCandidateCount_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionEntropy_isLittleO,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_sourceReductionEntropy_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_sourceReduction_guards,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_sourceReductionExtractor_flat}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-finite-sumset-construction}
  Set $L=\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil$. The actual candidate count is $o(n)$,
  the complete source entropy $k(n)$ is $o(n)$, and both numerical
  sampler guards eventually hold. Consequently the same fixed finite
  construction is eventually a sumset extractor at threshold $2^{k(n)}$
  and error $35/72$, with $k(n)\le n$ eventually.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Write $r=\lceil\log_2(L+1)\rceil+1$. The actual candidate seed width
  is at most $2^{41}r^3$, the growing depth at most $2^{42}r^3$, the
  matched guard overhead at most $2^{90}r^6$, and the full source entropy
  at most $2^{2^{44}r^3}$. A fixed polynomial in $\log L$ is $o(L)$;
  exponentiating a fixed polynomial in the iterated logarithm is $o(n)$.
  Transfer Gamma's eventual guard along $2^{24}L\to\infty$.
  The full candidate count is retained throughout.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Bounded evaluation and the single balanced hard family]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-uniform-hard-family}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionRuntime,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionEval,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionFamilyScale,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionBoundedCandidates,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionFamilyEval,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionFamily,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionHardEval,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionHardFamily,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionHardLanguage}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-finite-sumset-construction,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-extractor-padding}
  The runtime takes unary scale and enumeration counts and executes
  the actual sampler and affine calls, then XOR and majority.
  The length-indexed evaluator uses $L=\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil$, the
  exact outer count, and candidate count $\min(C,n+1)$. Its Boolean
  family is the unique output bit. XOR with a fresh first input bit
  gives the balanced hard evaluator, family, and accepted language.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The same computed family is polynomial-time and eventually extracts]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-uniform-hard-family}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionRuntime_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionRuntime_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionFamilyEval_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionFamily_eventually_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionFamily_eventually_flat,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionFamilyEntropy_isLittleO,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionHardFamily_succ,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionHardFamily_card_accepting,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionHardEval_ofFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionHardEval_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.mem_sourceReductionHardLanguage_ofFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.sourceReductionHardLanguage_mem_P}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-uniform-hard-family,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-sumset-construction-asymptotics,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-extractor-padding}
  Both evaluators belong to $\mathrm{FP}$ and compute their stated
  Boolean families at every length, including zero. The candidate cap
  is eventually inactive, so the unpadded computed family eventually
  equals the actual finite extractor with error $35/72$ and sublinear
  source entropy. The padded family is exactly balanced at every
  positive length, and its language belongs to $P$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The exact outer count is
  $(2^{\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil})^{2^{27}}$, so unary computation of
  rounded powers and fixed powers supplies its polynomial-time bound.
  Capped powers supply the candidate count on every input. Compose
  the certified runtimes over both bounded loops; source linearity and
  the canonical execution equations identify every actual call.
  Eventual cap inactivity transfers the statistical theorem to this
  same evaluator. Apply the checked balanced-padding and decision-function
  bridges.

  The constants are enormous: the polynomial has degree at least
  $2^{27}\approx 1.3\cdot 10^{8}$, and the cap is inactive at a positive
  length only once $\log_2(n+1)\ge 2^{27}\cdot 70^{3}\approx 4.6\cdot 10^{13}$.
  Every circuit bound for this family is therefore purely asymptotic, with
  an implicit threshold above $2^{4.6\cdot 10^{13}}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[A concrete polynomial-time family needs more than $(4-\varepsilon)n$ gates]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-four-n}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.sourceReductionHardFamily_eventually_lt_size}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-uniform-hard-family,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-padded-hardness}
  For every $\varepsilon>0$ and all sufficiently large $n$, every
  circuit computing the fixed family
  $\mathrm{sourceReductionHardFamily}(n)$ has more than
  $(4-\varepsilon)n$ gates. The basis contains all sixteen binary
  Boolean operations, repeated input slots and unrestricted fan-out
  are permitted, and every gate is counted. The family is fixed before
  $\varepsilon$, belongs to $P$, and $n$ is the full padded input length.
  No graph, extractor, entropy, or hard-family hypothesis remains.
  The circuit argument follows Ryan Williams's September 2026 working
  note and the author's circuit-to-read-once compiler; the constructed
  family uses Chattopadhyay--Liao's extractor route with the library's
  conservative finite parameters.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The dyadic support threshold has binary logarithm exactly $k(n)=o(n)$.
  Apply the checked balanced-padding cutwidth theorem at fixed error
  $35/72<1/2$, then shift its eventual statement from the unpadded index
  to the full input length.
\end{proof}



\begin{theorem}[Extraction with an approximately independent seed]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-perturbed-seed}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.perturbed_seed_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.perturbed_seed_reference_dist_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-average-leakage,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-conditional-distance}
  Let $p$ be the normalized law of $(T,X,S)$ with joint source masses
  bounded by nonnegative $\mu(t)$, and let $E$ be a threshold-$K$ strong
  extractor of nonnegative error $\varepsilon$. If
  $d(p,U_2p)\le\delta$, extraction retaining the actual $(T,S)$ marginal
  has error at most $\varepsilon+2\delta+K\sum_t\mu(t)$.
  Comparing instead with the uniformized output law for the independent-seed
  reference $U_2p$ costs only $\varepsilon+\delta+K\sum_t\mu(t)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply average leakage to the source marginal with a fresh uniform seed.
  Deterministic contraction transfers to the actual input law at cost
  $\delta$. Uniformizing the output contracts distance, so restoring the
  actual retained marginal costs at most one further $\delta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Head and tail structure of block sources]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-conditioning}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.exists_head_tail,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.isBlockSource_prepend,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.isBlockSource_of_prepend_mixture}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source-structure}
  Every threshold-$K$ source on $t+1$ blocks admits a normalized capped
  head law $w$ and normalized threshold-$K$ tail laws $p_a$ such that
  $p(a,z)=w(a)p_a(z)$ for all heads and tails. Conversely these conditions
  give a block source. More generally, nonnegative coefficients $c(h,a)$
  with row totals equal to a normalized capped head law give a block
  source by mixing the tail laws within each head row.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Normalize each positive head row. Assign a fixed positive row's
  conditional law to zero-mass heads; normalization guarantees such a row
  exists. Prefix-mass identities transfer every conditional cap. For the
  converse, sum the tail inequalities with the nonnegative row coefficients.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Correlated head repair with block-source tails]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-tail-repair}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_block_tail_replacement}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-coupling,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-conditioning,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Sample a latent value $a$ from a probability law $w$, and a conditional
  threshold-$K$ block-source tail $Z_a$. Given a deterministic head $f(a)$
  and any normalized threshold-$K$ head law $v$, there is a threshold-$K$
  block source $R$ with head marginal $v$ such that
  \[
    d\bigl((f(a),Z_a),R\bigr)=d(f(a),v).
  \]
  No independence between the latent value and its tail is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Replace the first marginal of the joint law of $(f(a),a)$, preserving
  the latent marginal and paying exactly the head distance. Attach the
  same normalized conditional tail to each latent value. Keeping all tags
  preserves the distance exactly; projection can only decrease it.
  The unchanged head distance gives the reverse inequality. The row-mixture
  criterion proves that the repaired tuple is a block source.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Successive pair splitting]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockIndexEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockEquiv}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source}
  Split a tuple of $t$ pairs into $2t$ coordinates, preserving the order
  first coordinate, second coordinate, then the next pair. With zero-based
  indexing, pair $i$ occupies positions $2i$ and $2i+1$. This map is an
  equivalence and introduces no randomness.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Splitting every block into consecutive halves]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsBlockSource.exists_split_pow_two,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockEquiv_apply_fst,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockEquiv_apply_snd}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-two-block-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-conditioning,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-tail-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Let $p$ be a threshold-$2^k$ block source on $t$ pairs of values in an
  alphabet of size $2^m$. If $s\le m$ and $m+s+e\le k$, then its split
  law is within distance $t2^{-e}$ of a threshold-$2^s$ source on $2t$
  consecutive blocks. The original pairs can be dependent, with the input
  entropy bound conditioned on all earlier pairs. This is the finite
  power-of-two form of Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao, Corollary 5.4.
  The zero-block case is included without an upper bound on $k$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Disintegrate the original head pair and its block-source tails. Repair
  the pair into a two-block source while retaining its original joint cap.
  Correlated head replacement therefore gives a nearby source that still
  has the original threshold on all pair blocks. Disintegrate it again,
  split each conditional tail by induction, and prepend the repaired head
  coordinates. Deterministic contraction charges at most $2^{-e}$ for the
  head repair, and normalized conditional averaging charges at most
  $(t-1)2^{-e}$ for the remaining pairs. The triangle inequality gives the
  stated error; the empty tuple case has distance zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Splitting with an earlier retained seed]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split-family}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_split_pow_two_seedFamily}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Under the preceding entropy and alphabet-size conditions, suppose that
  every fixing of an earlier uniform seed gives a threshold-$2^k$ source
  on $t$ pairs. Splitting the pairs while retaining that seed gives a joint
  law within $t2^{-e}$ of a uniform-seed family of threshold-$2^s$ sources
  on $2t$ blocks. The source may depend on the earlier seed, and splitting
  introduces no fresh seed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Choose the preceding splitting witness separately in each seed fiber.
  The seed-preserving deterministic map acts fiberwise, and exact distance
  averaging gives the same bound on the joint distribution.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Shared-seed block condensation]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-condenser}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.block}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-strong,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-conditioning,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-tail-repair,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Let $C$ be a weighted strong condenser from threshold $K_{\rm in}$ to
  $K_{\rm out}$ with error $\varepsilon$, and let $(X_1,\ldots,X_t)$ be a
  threshold-$K_{\rm in}$ block source. For one independent uniform seed $Y$,
  \[
    (Y,C(X_1,Y),\ldots,C(X_t,Y))
  \]
  is within distance $t\varepsilon$ of a uniform-seed family of normalized
  threshold-$K_{\rm out}$ block sources. The blocks can be dependent;
  the seed appears only once. This proves the finite shared-seed conclusion
  of Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao, Lemma 5.5.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct on the number of blocks, conditioning on the original head.
  Correct each conditional tail by the induction hypothesis; averaging
  and deterministic processing cost at most $t\varepsilon$. Apply the
  condenser to the head marginal, and repair the head separately at each
  seed while retaining its latent value. Exact distance averaging bounds
  this second change by $\varepsilon$. The triangle inequality gives
  $(t+1)\varepsilon$. The empty tuple case has distance zero.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Shared-seed block extraction]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.block}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-source-structure}
  Applying a weighted strong extractor of error $\varepsilon$ to all $t$
  blocks with the same uniform seed has discrepancy at most $t\varepsilon$
  from independent uniform seed and output tuple on every joint test.
  The input is a block source at the extractor's threshold. Seed and output
  alphabets are nonempty.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Regard the extractor as a condenser at full output cardinality. Its
  conditional block-source witnesses are exactly uniform on the whole
  output tuple by full-entropy uniformity. Use the distance-to-test bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[The scheduled condenser with one seed for all blocks]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-block-condenser}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decodedExplicitCondenser_block,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decodedExplicitCondenser_block_ofFn}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-programs}
  For positive rate $u$, apply the actual decoded scheduled condenser to
  every block of an $n$-bit threshold-$2^k$ block source, using one field
  seed. Its retained-seed output is within $t2^{-e}$ of a seed-conditioned
  threshold-$2^k$ block source. Both Boolean-vector inputs and arbitrary
  injective encodings into words of length $n$ are covered. This is a
  statistical theorem about the actual component maps; a uniform evaluator
  for the full multilevel recursion remains a separate construction.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Instantiate shared-seed block condensation with the proved weighted
  guarantee of the scheduled runtime condenser.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One pair of seeds for the one-shot leaves]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-blocks}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decodedOneShotExtractor_block,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.decodedOneShotExtractor_block_ofFn}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-programs}
  For a source of $t$ blocks at threshold $2^{\ell+2e}$, the actual one-shot
  map can reuse the same pair of independent field seeds on all blocks.
  The entire tuple of $t$ outputs of $\ell$ bits has joint test discrepancy
  at most $t2^{-e}$ from uniform, retaining the field-seed pair once.
  Both fixed-length Boolean-vector inputs and generic injective word
  encodings are supported, without extra capacity or positivity premises.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply shared-seed block extraction to the proved weighted one-shot
  guarantee. The finite field and output types supply the required
  nonempty instances.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Adding a fresh seed while retaining earlier randomness]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainSeedEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedStep}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-strong}
  Given a joint weighting on an earlier seed and a current state, sample
  one fresh independent uniform seed, apply the specified deterministic
  step, and retain the ordered pair of seeds with the output. The new seed
  is independent of the entire old joint law.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Transport and composition of retained-seed steps]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.seedFamilyWeight_product,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.weightDist_seedFamilyWeight_product,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedStep_seedFamilyWeight,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedStep_dist_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.IsProbabilityWeight.retainedSeedStep,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.retainedSeedStep_weightedSeededOutput}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-tagged-distance}
  Retained-seed steps contract total variation and preserve probability
  when the fresh seed type is nonempty. On an exact earlier-seed family,
  the resulting conditional law is the deterministic image in each pair
  of seed values. Distances over a product seed average the fresh-seed
  joint distances over earlier seeds. Successive seeded programs equal
  their deterministic composition with both seeds retained.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Reassociate the seed coordinates by an equivalence and use product
  cardinality, exact tagged averaging, and deterministic contraction.
  Composition follows from composition of the finite pushforwards.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[One condense-and-split level with accumulated error]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-condense-split-level}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.condenseSplit}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-condenser,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split-family}
  Suppose the actual joint law of earlier seeds and $t$ current blocks
  is within $\eta$ of an earlier-seed family of threshold-$K$ block sources.
  Let a fresh-seed condenser preserve those blocks at output threshold
  $2^k$, with error $\varepsilon$ and pair-valued output over an alphabet
  of size $2^m$. If $s\le m$ and $m+s+e\le k$, condensing with one fresh
  seed shared by all blocks and then splitting gives a threshold-$2^s$
  block-source family at joint error at most
  $\eta+t(\varepsilon+2^{-e})$. Both earlier and fresh seeds are retained.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply block condensation separately at each earlier seed, averaging its
  joint fresh-seed error. Split the ideal pair-block family and contract
  the condenser comparison through the same split map. The preceding
  approximation contracts through the whole step. Two triangle inequalities
  add the three errors.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Final extraction with all earlier seeds retained]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-block-extraction}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.retained_block,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededExtractor.retained_block_tests}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-extractor}
  If the current joint law is $\eta$-close to a family of $t$ block sources
  at an extractor's threshold, a fresh-seed extractor of error
  $\varepsilon$, shared across the blocks, produces a joint law within
  $\eta+t\varepsilon$ of uniform on both seed coordinates and the entire
  output tuple. For normalized actual weights, the same bound holds on
  every joint test. The earlier and fresh seed types and output alphabet
  are nonempty.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Full-entropy block-condenser witnesses are uniform on all output tuples.
  Average their distance bounds over earlier seeds, and use contraction
  for the preceding joint error. Equal total mass gives the test bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Finite recursive block maps]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.RecursiveSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockCount,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockMap,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockError,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-block-split,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-seed-step}
  Starting from an initial seeded map, run $h$ levels, each using its own
  fresh seed to condense all current blocks and split their pair outputs.
  Block alphabets and seed types can vary with the level. Starting from
  $t$ blocks, level $i$ has $2^it$ blocks. The recursive extractor starts
  with a single condensed block and finally applies one shared-seed
  extractor to every leaf. Every seed is retained exactly once.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite recursive block-source guarantee]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-source}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.exists_recursiveBlockSource,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockCount_eq}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-condense-split-level}
  Suppose the joint law of the initial seed and output is within $\eta$
  of a uniform-seed family of threshold-$2^{k_0}$ sources on $t$ blocks.
  At each executed level $i<h$, let the condenser
  preserve threshold $2^{k_i}$ with error $\varepsilon_i$, let its half-output
  alphabet have size $2^{m_i}$, and suppose
  \[
    k_{i+1}\le m_i,\qquad m_i+k_{i+1}+e_i\le k_i.
  \]
  The actual recursive map is within
  \[
    \eta+\sum_{i<h}2^it(\varepsilon_i+2^{-e_i})
  \]
  of a retained-seed family of threshold-$2^{k_h}$ sources on $2^ht$ blocks.
  No bound on each individual seed's error is assumed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct on the number of levels, applying the preceding level theorem to
  the actual recursive output. Exact seeded composition identifies its
  output with the next recursive map. The error recurrence is the displayed
  finite sum. Only hypotheses for the executed levels are used.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact seed cardinalities and finite error budget]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-budget}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveSeeds_card,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveSeeds_card_pow_two,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockError_const,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockError_dyadic_total,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockError_dyadic_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map}
  The retained seed alphabet has cardinality equal to the initial seed
  cardinality times the product of the $h$ fresh-seed cardinalities.
  Hence power-of-two seed widths add. With one initial block and all
  initial, local, splitting, and final extraction errors budgeted by
  $2^{-E}$, the total bound is
  $(3\cdot2^h-1)2^{-E}$. Choosing $E=e+h+2$ makes this at most $2^{-e}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct using product cardinality for the seed formula. Sum the doubled
  block counts for the local errors, and add the initial and leaf errors.
  The inequality $3\cdot2^h-1\le4\cdot2^h$ gives the dyadic budget.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Strong extraction by the finite recursive composition]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.singleton,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.recursiveExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.WeightedStrongSeededCondenser.recursiveExtractor_dyadic}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-source,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-budget,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-retained-block-extraction}
  Combine an initial strong condenser, the lossless level condensers
  satisfying the preceding finite entropy inequalities, and a final strong
  extractor at threshold $2^{k_h}$. The actual recursive function is a
  weighted strong extractor at the initial input threshold, with error
  at most the sum of the initial bound, the level budgets, and $2^h$ times
  the final component bound. In particular, local error exponent $e+h+2$
  gives final error at most $2^{-e}$. These are finite guarantees for the
  supplied components; no asymptotic schedule or uniform runtime claim is
  included in this theorem.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Transport the initial condenser witness to a single-coordinate block
  source. Apply the recursive guarantee, then final retained-seed block
  extraction. Exact seeded composition identifies the tested function with
  the recursive extractor. The finite dyadic budget gives the specialization.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-seed additivity of recursive maps]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-additivity}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.splitBlockEquiv_add,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.condenseSplitMap_add,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockMap_add,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockExtractor_add}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map}
  If the initial map and every executed condenser preserve addition for
  each fixed seed, so does the recursive block map. If the final component
  also preserves addition, the recursive extractor does too. Only addition
  structures and the stated component equalities are needed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Successive splitting is a coordinate rearrangement, so it preserves
  pointwise addition. Induct through the level maps and compose with the
  final component equality at each output coordinate.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[A constant-rate recursive parameter schedule]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockWidth,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockSeedWidth,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockReserve}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-condenser}
  For depth $h$, leaf reserve $Q$, and common error exponent $E$, set
  $k_i=4^{h-i}Q$. Starting from width $N$, recursively set $n_{i+1}$ to
  the actual half-output width of the rate-three condenser on $n_i$ bits
  at entropy $k_i$ and exponent $E$. Record its actual field seed width
  $b_i$. The explicit reserve is $Q=4096(L+E+1)$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite width, entropy, and work bounds]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_last,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_succ,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockWidth_bounds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlock_split_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockSeedWidth_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlock_payload_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlock_count_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-split-budget}
  Write $T$ for the condenser budget on width $N$, entropy $k_0$, and
  exponent $E$. If $k_0\le N$ and $3(24T)+6E\le2Q$, then every executed
  level satisfies $k_{i+1}\le n_{i+1}$ and
  $n_{i+1}+k_{i+1}+E\le k_i$. For $i\le h$, one has
  $k_i\le n_i\le N$, $b_i\le24T$, and $2^in_i\le2N$.
  Also $k_h=Q$ and $k_i=4k_{i+1}$ for $i<h$.
  When $Q>0$, the block count satisfies $2^i\le k_0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct on the level, using monotonicity of the condenser budget and
  the rate-three splitting inequality. The geometric entropy decrease
  bounds the width and total payload; positivity of $Q$ bounds the count.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[An explicit reserve pays all rounded split budgets]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-reserve}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockReserve_pos,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockEntropy_zero_clog_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockInitialWidth_bounds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockInitialBudget_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.recursiveBlockReserve_budget}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-pair-condenser}
  Suppose $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L$. Set $Q=4096(L+E+1)$,
  $k_0=4^hQ$, and let $N$ be the full output width of the actual rate-one
  condenser on $n$ bits at entropy $k_0$. Then $Q>0$,
  $k_0\le N\le64k_0$, and its next condenser budget satisfies
  \[
    T\le E+4h+2\lceil\log_2(L+E+1)\rceil+64.
  \]
  If also $h\le L$, then $3(24T)+6E\le2Q$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound the logarithm of $4^hQ+1$ by
  $2h+13+\lceil\log_2(L+E+1)\rceil$. The checked rounded field and
  output-rate bounds give the width and budget estimates. The reserve's
  coefficient absorbs the remaining integer slack.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The fully specified scheduled block extractor]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockInitialWidth,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.ScheduledInitialSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.ScheduledLevelSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.ScheduledFinalSeed,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockInitial,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockStep,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedBits}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-map,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-parameters,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-program}
  Start with the actual rate-one condenser, run the scheduled rate-three
  paired condensers for $h$ levels, and apply the actual one-shot extractor
  with output length $\ell$ to all leaves. Its seed consists of one initial
  field seed, one fresh field seed per level, and one final field-seed pair
  shared by every leaf. Let $d$ be the sum of their field bit widths.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Strong extraction by the specified schedule]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractor_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractor_dyadic}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-reserve,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-weighted-programs}
  If $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L$ and $h\le L$, set
  $E=e+h+2$ and $Q=4096(L+E+1)$. The specified map is a weighted strong
  extractor from $n$-bit sources of min-entropy at least $4^hQ$ to
  $2^hL$ bits, with error at most $2^{-e}$. More generally, the global
  reserve budget and $\ell+2E\le Q$ give error
  $(3\cdot2^h-1)2^{-E}$ for leaf output length $\ell$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Instantiate the finite recursive extraction theorem with the actual
  initial, internal, and leaf maps. The explicit reserve pays every
  splitting inequality and the leaf extractor's entropy requirement.
  The dyadic error budget gives the stated final error. This follows the
  recursive method of Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao,
  \href{https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2021/075/}{Theorem 5.6 of
  \emph{Affine Extractors for Almost Logarithmic Entropy}} (2021), with our
  coarser factor-four entropy schedule rather than their sharper bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact and bounded seed size of the specified schedule]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-seeds}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.card_scheduledBlockSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockSeedBits_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-budget,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-reserve,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-parameters}
  The actual retained seed type has cardinality $2^d$ for all natural
  parameters. Under $\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil\le L$ and $h\le L$, with
  $Q=4096(L+E+1)$ and leaf output length $L$, its width satisfies
  \[
    d\le8192\bigl(L+(h+1)(E+h+
      \lceil\log_2(L+E+1)\rceil+1)\bigr).
  \]
  This is a finite bound on the specified seed type.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Multiply the proved field cardinalities along the actual seed tuple.
  Bound the initial seed by its input budget, every level seed by the
  common compressed budget, and the final shared pair by the one-shot
  estimates. Sum these bounds once per retained seed.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fixed-seed XOR law of the specified extractor]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-linearity}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.scheduledBlockExtractor_xor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-recursive-block-additivity,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-one-shot-program}
  For every fixed complete seed tuple and all natural parameters, the
  scheduled extractor sends XOR of two Boolean inputs to coordinatewise
  addition of their outputs in $\mathbf F_2$. No entropy or error-budget
  premise is needed for this identity.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Apply recursive additivity to the actual condenser, its serialization
  into Boolean halves, and the actual one-shot leaf extractor.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The fixed polylogarithmic-output family]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-polylog-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockLength,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockDepth,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockErrorExponent,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockReserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockEntropy,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockSeedBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockOutputBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.PolylogBlockSeeds,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor}
  Fix natural parameters $a,e$. At input length $n$, take
  $L=\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil$, raw depth
  $h=a\lceil\log_2(L+1)\rceil$, $E=e+h+2$, and
  $Q=4096(L+E+1)$. Instantiate the specified extractor with leaf length
  $L$, source entropy threshold $k=4^hQ$, and its actual retained seeds.
  These family definitions do not truncate the depth at small lengths.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Logarithmic seed, sublinear entropy, and polylogarithmic output]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-polylog-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_polylogBlockDepth_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.polylogBlockEntropy_isLittleO,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_polylogBlockEntropy_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_polylogBlockSeedBits_le,

  weighted strong extractor at source support threshold $2^k$ and error
  $2^{-e}$. Its entropy threshold satisfies $k=o(n)$, so eventually $k\le n$.
  Its actual seed alphabet has cardinality $2^d$, with eventually
  $d\le16384L$. At every length its output length $M$ satisfies
  \[
    L^{a+1}\le M\le2^a(L+1)^{a+1}.

  near-linear entropy dependence in Chattopadhyay--Goodman--Liao,
  Theorem 5.6. The quantifiers here fix both $a$ and $e$.

  \leanok
  Apply the finite schedule and seed bounds once the raw depth fits $L$.
  Ceiling-logarithm estimates give the output bounds. Exponential growth
  dominates every fixed polynomial in the input logarithm, giving sublinear
  entropy and absorbing the depth-dependent seed terms into $16384L$.
\end{proof}

  $h=\min(r,\lceil\log_2(n+1)\rceil)$, $E=e+h+2$, the explicit leaf
  reserve, and the actual rate-one initial compressed width.
\end{definition}

  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-state}

  transition computes the prescribed widths and entropies through every

  computes its initial entropy by a bounded-power operation without a
  circular capacity premise. Its selected width contains that entropy
  and is at most $64$ times it, and its reserve pays the global split budget.
  The initialized numerical loop is polynomial-time uniformly up to any
  requested level clipped to $h$.

  \leanok
  Compose the unary half-width and bounded arithmetic certificates.
  Division by four matches the entropy recurrence through depth $h$.
  The independent bound on $4^h$ initializes the entropy; the explicit

\end{proof}

  to Boolean words of their exact summed field widths. These semantic

\end{definition}

  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-codec}

  Decoding each encoding recovers its tuple; encoding a decoded word

\end{theorem}

  inverse laws. Taking and dropping at those widths recover each adjacent
  component, including all coefficient zeros.

  consumes its first seed word. Each later step consumes one fresh seed
  prefix, applies the actual shared-seed condenser to every block, and
  keeps its two halves consecutively. The loop executes $h$ such steps.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform bounded execution of the complete payload loop]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-run}

  compressed width and $s$ is the original seed word. The entire initial
  compression and payload loop is uniformly polynomial-time when the
  numerical operands, including initial entropy, have unary certificates.
\end{theorem}

  \leanok

  Sum the proved widths of the numerical workspace, unary count, payload,
  and remaining seeds. The finite schedule bounds these throughout the

\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exact semantic correctness of every runtime level]

  $i\le h$ followed by any trailing word, the runtime state after $i$
  steps has exactly the prescribed width, entropy, and block count.
  Its payload is precisely the serialized recursive block tuple, in
  coordinate order, and its remaining seeds are exactly that trailing word.
  At $i=h$ this is the complete semantic leaf tuple with entropy $Q$.
  No entropy-budget or field-enumeration premise is required.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok

  condenser word. Induct using the prefix append law and the one-level
  runtime correctness theorem, keeping the trailing word arbitrary.
\end{proof}

  \leanok

  All projections and component programs are total on malformed encodings.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[The actual Boolean program is a linear strong extractor]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-boolean}

  budget and $\ell+2E\le Q$, the source threshold is $2^{4^hQ}$ and the
  error is at most $(3\cdot2^h-1)2^{-E}$. The explicit reserve specialization
  of Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-scheduled-extractor} has error
  at most $2^{-e}$. No caller-supplied finite-field instances are required.

  \leanok

\end{proof}

  condenser preserving every source cap.

\begin{proof}
  \leanok

  For seed padding, apply the retained-seed identity step to both actual
  and ideal laws; total variation cannot increase. The unchanged source

\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[A near-halving entropy schedule]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockRate,

    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockSeedBits}
  \leanok
  For depth $h$, reserve $Q$, and error exponent $E$, let
  \[
    u=16(h+1),\qquad
    k_i=2^{h-i}(8h+8+h-i)Q,\qquad \ell=(8h+7)Q.
  \]
  Starting at input width $N$, recursively use the actual rounded paired
  condenser width at entropy $k_i$, rate $u$, and exponent $E$.
  The seed length sums one field seed per level and the final one-shot
  condenser/hash seed pair. Initialization is the identity and needs no bits.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Finite near-halving budgets]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlock_split_budget_of_bound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlock_payload_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockSeedBits_le_of_bound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockOutputBits_accounting,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockOutputBits_lower}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-parameters}
  Suppose $k_0\le N$ and $T$ bounds the initial explicit condenser budget.
  The sufficient inequality $12(u+1)T+4E\le Q$ pays every splitting
  condition, bounds all payloads by $2N$, and bounds total seed bits by
  \[
    6h(u+1)T+84(T+1)+18\ell+24E+6.
  \]
  With $M=2^h\ell$, the exact identity $9M+2^hQ=8k_0$ and inequality
  $7k_0\le8M$ hold for every $h,Q$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Induct on the actual widths, using the entropy reserve and the sparse
  field rounding bounds at each level. Sum the level seed costs and add
  the final one-shot pair. The output accounting is direct integer algebra.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Strong near-halving extraction]

\end{proof}

    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockErrorExponent,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockReserve,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.GammaBlockSizeGuard,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockSeedBits,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-extractor}
  For target $b$, set $L=\lceil\log_2(b+1)\rceil$,
  $R=\lceil\log_2(L+1)\rceil$, $h=\lfloor\log_2 b\rfloor-3R-15$
  using natural subtraction, $E=h+4$, and
  \[
    Q=\left\lceil\frac{b}{2^h(8h+7)}\right\rceil.
  \]
  The guard is $3R+15\le\lfloor\log_2 b\rfloor$.
  Use $N=8b$ input bits and keep the first $b$ output bits.
  Output coverage holds even when the guard fails.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Gamma parameter validity and eventuality]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-parameters}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlock_output_ge,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockInputEntropy_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlock_split_budget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockSeedBits_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_gammaBlockSizeGuard}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-parameters}
  Under the guard, $k_0\le2b$, $M\ge b$, every reserve condition holds,
  and the actual total seed length satisfies $d\le2^{27}L^3$.
  The guard holds for all sufficiently large $b$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound the rounded reserve below by $4096(L+1)^2$ and the initial
  condenser budget above by $13(L+1)$. The depth offset pays the reserve
  and the ceiling loss while keeping $k_0\le2b$.
  The established logarithm-of-logarithm growth bound proves eventuality.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[An actual ordinary Gamma extractor]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-extractor}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractor_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractor_flat,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockSeedBits_bound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_gammaBlockExtractor_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_gammaBlockExtractor_flat}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-parameters,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-strong-projection-padding}
  Under the guard, the specified map on $8b$ input bits is a weighted
  strong seeded extractor at support threshold $2^{2b}$, output length $b$,
  and error $1/4$. Its seed length is at most $2^{27}L^3$.
  Consequently it is an ordinary flat seeded extractor with these
  parameters, for every sufficiently large $b$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Use the near-halving dyadic guarantee with $e=2$, increase the threshold
  to $2^{2b}$, and truncate to $b$ coordinates. Forget the retained seed
  to obtain the ordinary flat contract.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Near-halving seed words and complete runtime]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockSeedEquiv,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.NearHalvingBlockRunState,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockRun,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockFinish,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockExtractorBits}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-extractor}
  Encode level seeds in order, followed by the shared final condenser and
  hash seeds. The workspace records width, remaining depth and its power
  of two, block count, payload, and unused seed suffix. Each actual
  condenser step halves the stored remaining power and doubles the count.
  The final extractor reads both of its seeds at their exact widths.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform near-halving evaluation and exact correctness]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockRun_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockExtractorBits_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockRun_eq_recursiveBlockMap,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockExtractorBits_eq_nearHalvingBlockExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.nearHalvingBlockExtractorBits_append_eq_nearHalvingBlockBooleanExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockDepth_pow_le,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockDepth_pow_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockSizeGuard_fpPred}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-program,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-parameters}
  The complete program is polynomial-time for uniformly unary-computable
  parameters and initialization power, when the number of iterations is at
  most $h$ and is either zero or satisfies the capacity and reserve bounds.
  At $h$ iterations and the specified leaf length, its output equals the
  statistical map on every canonical seed word, including arbitrary
  trailing bits. Correctness needs no entropy premise.
  For Gamma parameters, $2^h\le b+1$ on every input, and all parameter
  generators and the size-guard decision have uniform certificates.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Bound every encoded state, including payload and unary counters, before
  bounded iteration. Compose with the shared one-shot block program.
  Induction identifies all runtime state fields with the recursive map;
  exact seed slicing gives the final output identity. Capped powering and
  the unconditional depth bound certify Gamma initialization.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The total Gamma evaluator]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractorRuntime,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractorEval}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-parameters,
    def:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-program}
  Generate the rounded parameters from $b$ and execute $h$ levels if the
  size guard holds, or zero levels otherwise. Return the first $b$ bits
  on the guarded branch and $b$ zero bits otherwise. The single paired
  string evaluator infers $b$ as the source-word length divided by eight.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[One total polynomial-time Gamma program]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-program}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractorRuntime_length,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractorRuntime_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractorEval_mem_FP,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractorRuntime_append_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockExtractorEval_pair_append_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_gammaBlockExtractorEval}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-program,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-near-halving-program,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-parameters}
  The runtime always emits exactly $b$ bits and is uniformly
  polynomial-time in unary $b$, source, and seed words. The paired
  evaluator is in $\mathrm{FP}$ on every encoded word.
  Under the size guard, it computes the named Gamma extractor on every
  $8b$-bit source and every exact-length seed, with any trailing padding.
  This agreement therefore holds eventually for the whole family.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  The guarded iteration count satisfies the resource bound on both
  branches before selecting the final output. Compose the unconditional
  parameter and bounded-run certificates. Exact near-halving program
  correctness and prefix truncation identify the output on the guard;
  source length divided by eight recovers $b$ on all canonical inputs.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[The actual Gamma program on a fixed seed budget]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-padded}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockSeedBudget,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockPaddedExtractor}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-program}
  Give the runtime an $8b$-bit source and a seed of exactly
  $r(b)=2^{27}\lceil\log_2(b+1)\rceil^3$ bits. Read its $b$ output
  coordinates to define the Boolean family. This definition invokes the
  actual total string program, including its specified small-size behavior.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform Gamma extraction on every padded seed word]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-padded}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockSeedBudget_unaryFn,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockPaddedExtractor_eval,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockPaddedExtractor_eq,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockPaddedExtractor_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.gammaBlockPaddedExtractor_flat,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_gammaBlockPaddedExtractor_weighted,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Extractor.eventually_gammaBlockPaddedExtractor_flat}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-padded,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-program,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-gamma-extractor,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-strong-projection-padding}
  The budget $r(b)$ is uniformly unary-computable. The same single
  $\mathrm{FP}$ evaluator computes the entire Boolean output at every
  size and seed word. Under the size guard, the family is a weighted
  strong extractor with input length $8b$, threshold $2^{2b}$, output
  length $b$, and error $1/4$, retaining all $r(b)$ uniform seed bits.
  Its ordinary flat guarantee consequently holds for all sufficiently
  large $b$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Split each padded word at the actual seed length. The runtime identity
  with an arbitrary trailing suffix gives the original semantic Gamma
  output on its prefix. Independent seed padding preserves strong
  extraction, while the exact output length identifies every coordinate
  with the paired string evaluator, including below the size guard.
\end{proof}

The fixed-family logarithmic-seed primitive and the ordinary Gamma
linear-output parameters are now supplied by actual recursive maps.
The complete actual affine correlation breaker now has an original-source
security theorem for every positive tampering count, explicit finite
parameters and dyadic error, and a total uniform polynomial-time evaluator.
The actual first phase initializes the repeated-round invariant, whose
entropy losses and error are discharged by the chosen global schedule.
The actual sumset/amplification composition and fixed-family parity estimates
are proved above. Their eventual sublinear entropy and bounded uniform evaluator
give the concrete polynomial-time family in
Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-four-n}.

\begin{definition}[Constraint networks, realized patterns, and determining sets]
  \label{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-network}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.Computes,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.pastSet,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.futureSet,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.realized,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.Determines,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.charged}
  \leanok
  A \emph{constraint network} on $n$ variables is a multigraph whose vertices
  carry local checks on the bits written on their incident edges, with a port
  (a vertex and an incident edge) for every variable it reads. It computes
  $f$ when the accepted inputs are exactly those admitting an edge assignment
  that satisfies every check and carries the input on every port. For a
  prefix $L$ of a vertex ordering and a cut assignment $\sigma$, the
  \emph{past set} consists of the assignments to the variables read in $L$
  that extend to an edge assignment satisfying the checks of $L$ and agreeing
  with $\sigma$ on the cut; the \emph{future set} is defined symmetrically.
  The patterns \emph{realized} on an edge set $C$ are the restrictions to $C$
  of satisfying edge assignments. A subset $T$ \emph{determines} $C$ when two
  satisfying assignments that agree on $T$ agree on $C$. The edges
  \emph{charged} at a vertex are the cut before it and its incident edges.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Cut counting]
  \label{lem:lowerbounds-algebraic-cut-counting}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.accepted_of_mem_pastSet_of_mem_futureSet,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.card_realized_le_of_determines,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.card_accepting_le_of_realized,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Network.card_accepting_le}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-network}
  Let a network compute a $K$-rectangle-free function $f$, $K>1$, and fix a
  vertex ordering. If a subset $T\subseteq C$ determines $C$, at most
  $2^{|T|}$ patterns are realized on $C$. If at most $P$ patterns are
  realized on the edges charged at every vertex, then either the final past
  set is small, so that fewer than $K\cdot 2^{n-n'}$ inputs are accepted,
  where $n'$ variables are read, or
  \[
    |f^{-1}(1)| \le |V|\cdot P\cdot (K-1)^2 .
  \]
  With every prefix cut of size at most $w$ and every degree at most three,
  $P=2^{w+3}$ is admissible.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Gluing a past and a future assignment of one cut assignment yields an
  accepted input, so the past and future sets form a one-rectangle and one
  of them has fewer than $K$ elements. Charge every accepted input to the
  first vertex at which the past set of its satisfying assignment reaches
  size $K$; the past set before that vertex and the future set after it are
  both small. The key of an input is that vertex and the realized pattern on
  its charged edges, which contain both cuts and the ports read at the
  vertex, so at most $(K-1)^2$ inputs share a key and there are at most
  $|V|\cdot P$ keys. Restriction to a determining subset is injective on
  realized patterns.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The wiring network and its generators]
  \label{lem:lowerbounds-algebraic-wiring-generators}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.Wiring.network_computes,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Wiring.maxDegreeLE_three,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Wiring.connected,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Wiring.card_vertex_le_two_mul_size,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Wiring.card_edge_sub_card_vertex,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Wiring.determines_of_generators,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Wiring.card_realized_le_two_pow_generators,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.Wiring.card_accepting_le_of_generators}
  \leanok
  \uses{def:lowerbounds-algebraic-network,
    lem:lowerbounds-algebraic-cut-counting}
  The wiring network of a circuit over the full binary basis has a vertex for
  every wire with a path to the output and, for a signal feeding $f\ge2$
  slots, $f-1$ copy vertices of degree three; gate vertices check their
  operation, the output gate checks the value $1$, and copy vertices check
  agreement. It computes the circuit's function, is connected with maximum
  degree three, has at most $2s+1$ vertices, and its edges minus vertices
  equal the reachable gates minus the reachable inputs. Call a signal carried
  by an edge set $C$ a \emph{generator} of $C$ unless it is a gate both of
  whose argument signals are carried by $C$. One edge of $C$ per generator
  determines $C$, so at most $2^{|\mathrm{generators}(C)|}$ patterns are
  realized on $C$, and the cut-counting bound holds with $P=2^{w}$ whenever
  the edges charged at every vertex have at most $w$ generators. No layout
  theorem bounding generators is proved; the assembly below charges edges.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Under a satisfying assignment every edge of one signal carries the bit of
  the signal's first outgoing edge, by the copy checks. Induct along the
  program order: a generator's bit is fixed by its chosen edge, and a
  determined gate carries its operation applied to two bits that already
  agree. The counting statements are instances of
  Lemma~\ref{lem:lowerbounds-algebraic-cut-counting}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Cutwidth bound for rectangle-free families]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_pathwidthBound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_rectangleFree}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bisection-pathwidth,
    lem:lowerbounds-algebraic-cut-counting,
    lem:lowerbounds-algebraic-wiring-generators}
  Every rectangle-free Boolean function family with polynomial rectangle
  threshold and at least $2^{n-2}$ accepting inputs, as in
  Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-nechiporuk}, needs circuits of more
  than $(4-\varepsilon)n$ gates over the full binary basis (all sixteen
  binary gates, every gate counted), for every $\varepsilon>0$ and all large
  $n$. The cubic graph bounds are proved, so the theorem has no graph
  hypothesis. This reusable theorem applies to every family satisfying
  the displayed properties. The concrete family of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-explicit-four-n} uses its
  subexponential-threshold form.
  The circuit argument follows Ryan Williams's private working note of
  September 2026, combining the rectangle bound for read-once programs
  with the circuit-to-read-once compiler of the author's counting note.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Order the gates by a path decomposition of the circuit's wiring graph and
  count the rectangles cut at each position.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Subexponential-threshold cutwidth bound]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth-subexponential}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_pathwidthBound_of_log_sublinear,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_pathwidthBound_of_flatSumsetExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_bisectionBound_of_log_sublinear,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_bisectionBound_of_flatSumsetExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_flatSumsetExtractor_of_log_sublinear}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-extractor-hardness,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-bisection-pathwidth}
  The polynomial threshold in Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth}
  can be replaced by $\log_2 K(n)=o(n)$. In particular, a family of flat-source
  sumset extractors with this threshold and fixed error at most $1/4$
  satisfies the same circuit lower bound with no graph hypothesis.
  The explicit sumset family and its uniform polynomial-time evaluation
  are supplied above with sublinear source entropy. Li's Theorem 7.13 (2023) provides
  logarithmic source entropy; Chattopadhyay and Liao (2021) already provide
  polylogarithmic source entropy, which is sufficient for this version.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Keep the terms involving $\log_2 K$ in the finite counting inequality.
  The loss is $O(\log n+\log K)=o(n)$, so the coefficient remains four.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Cutwidth bound for nondeterministic circuits]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth-nondet}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_of_pathwidthBound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_of_log_sublinear,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_of_flatSumsetExtractor,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_of_rectangleFree,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_of_flatSumsetExtractor_of_log_sublinear}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth-subexponential}
  Under the hypotheses of Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth},
  the same bound holds for nondeterministic circuits: for every
  $\varepsilon > 0$ and all large $n$, every circuit on $n + m$ inputs with
  arbitrary $m$, whose accepted inputs $x$ are exactly those with some witness $y$
  making the output $1$ has more than $(4 - \varepsilon)n$ gates.
  Nondeterminism does not reduce the size below this bound. The subexponential
  threshold of Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth-subexponential}
  is also sufficient.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Witness inputs are read by the wiring graph but carry no port of the
  computed function, so forgetting their ports (\texttt{Network.forget})
  leaves a network with the same multigraph computing the existential
  projection. The excess of the wiring graph counts every reachable input,
  ordinary or witness, so it is at most the gate count minus the ordinary
  inputs read. Every signal except the output feeds a slot; charging signals
  and copy vertices to slots gives at most $2s+1$ wiring vertices, independent
  of the declared witness count. The deterministic assembly then applies.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Cutwidth bound with a general ordering coefficient]
  \label{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth-coefficient}
  \lean{Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_orderingBound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_cutwidthBound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_of_pathwidthCoefficient,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_of_orderingBound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_of_cutwidthBound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_of_pathwidthCoefficient,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.eventually_lt_size_balancePad_of_flatSumsetExtractor_of_orderingBound,
    Algebraic.Cutwidth.nondet_eventually_lt_size_balancePad_of_flatSumsetExtractor_of_orderingBound}
  \leanok
  \uses{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-ordering-bound,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth-subexponential,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth-nondet,
    thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-padded-hardness}
  Let $A>0$, and suppose that for every $\eta>0$ some $C$ satisfies
  $\mathrm{OrderingBound}(A,\eta,C)$. Every family as in
  Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-cutwidth-subexponential} then needs
  more than $(1+1/A-\varepsilon)n$ gates, for deterministic circuits and for
  nondeterministic circuits with arbitrarily many witness inputs, for every
  $\varepsilon>0$ and all large $n$. The balanced padding of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:lowerbounds-algebraic-padded-hardness} needs more than
  $(1+1/A-\varepsilon)(n+1)$ gates. A cubic cutwidth or pathwidth
  coefficient $c>0$ at every positive slack gives the circuit coefficient
  $1+1/(2c)$. The coefficient-four theorems are the case $A=1/3$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  \leanok
  Let $\theta=\min\{\varepsilon,1/A\}$ and use the ordering slack
  $\eta=A^2\theta/2$. A circuit with at most $(1+1/A-\theta)n$ gates that
  reads more than $n-k$ inputs, $k=\lceil\log_2K\rceil$, has excess at most
  $(1/A-\theta)n+k$, and $(A+\eta)(1/A-\theta)\le1-A\theta/2$. Comparing
  the $2^{n-2}$ accepted inputs with the cut-counting bound leaves
  $A\theta n/2\le O(\log n+\log K)$, which fails for all large $n$.
\end{proof}
